- Capabilities name the browser vendor the server picked: `tools[].key` for `browser` is the key row of the vendor picked or resolved (`kernel` where Kernel is picked or resolved, else `browserbase`), so a page links a missing Kernel key to Kernel's row, and `features.browser` is the vendor the browser resolves to (`browserbase` or `kernel`), else `none`.
  - A run made before the browser was pickable, opened again while Browserbase's keys are missing, reads its browser unavailable for that launch only: its kept choice stays Browserbase, so keys restored later make its next launch run there. Before, the refusal was kept and no key restored it; a run that kept it so reads as Browserbase (or Kernel, for Kernel's refusal) again at its next launch, saved back. A run that resolved to no browser at its first launch (`auto` with no key) or to `chrome` stays as it was.
- A run whose model provider stops the answer on its content filter now ends `failed` with `error.code` `moderated` and
  says so in plain words: "The model provider stopped this answer (its content filter). Rephrase the request, or try
  another model." Before, it ended `run_failed` with the provider's jargon ("Response incomplete: content_filter"). The
  Timeline's error row keeps the harness's words for the stop (pi-ai's on pi, the kernel's on claude-agent and codex). On pi the run reads pi-ai's words for the stop on each API. On
  claude-agent and codex the session's model proxy reads the provider's stop (Anthropic's `refusal`, an OpenAI answer
  `incomplete` for `content_filter`) and the harness ends the turn at once: the CLI no longer asks for the refused answer
  again, turn after turn. Only the turn's own answer request ends it: a side call the CLI makes beside the turn (one
  without the session's tools) that the provider stops is logged and left to the CLI. The refused request is booked at
  the usage its end reports. The kernel exports `CONTENT_FILTERED`, the text such a turn ends with; a provider policy may
  say a stop through `filtered` and a side call through `answers`, and the model proxy tells its machine through
  `onFiltered`.
- **A Daytona box's host list holds after a restart, and an odd read of it never leaves a sandbox behind**
  - serve: a run's Daytona box found at its next call is kept only when its domain list is the one `AGENTRUN_BOX_ALLOW` names now (the same hosts, in any order). A box made under a wider list, or one whose list Daytona does not say, is destroyed and made again, so a narrowed setting holds for a run whose server restarted.
  - pi-transport: a box's facts carry its hosts in `allow`. A `domainAllowList` read back as a list of hosts is taken; any value that is neither a string nor a list deletes the sandbox and refuses the create as `unsupported`, where it threw before and left the sandbox.
  - serve: a Daytona box with no network after Daytona refused its list says why in `network_reason` (`reason` stays for an unavailable box), and the log line gives Daytona's own words (`event=box_network ... message="..."`).
- A decision receipt keeps its request's error whole up to 64 KB (it was cut at 2000 characters), marking a longer one `error_truncated`. A workflow run's graph redacts a decision's error before it cuts it, and holds back the tail of one stored cut (marked, or exactly 2000 characters from before), as it does a call's, so a held key across the cut shows no part.
- A decision's row in a run's store keeps its receipt's `error_truncated`, so a completed run's decision error stored cut at 64 KB is still held back at its tail on the graph, where a held value the cut split would sit.
- A workflow run's decision rows on the Timeline hold no held key: a provider's error, a source's title, an answer or a refused question that quotes one shows `[redacted]`. Each row's summary and text are redacted whole before they are cut, and an error stored cut has its last characters held back, where a key the cut split would sit. A key saved after a workflow run ended is redacted from its decision rows on the next read.
- `GET /v2/capabilities`' `features.browser` is `browserbase` when `tools[]` lists `browser` as available, and `none` when it does not. It was always `none`, so a client reading `features` hid a browser the server runs.
- The parts of every workflow run's graph that serve keeps redacted, and each run's last whole graph kept for a busy store's read, share one cache bounded at 32 MB, which evicts the least recently used entries one at a time. It used to keep up to 2048 parts per run with no bound across runs, and dropped them all at once past that.
- A workflow run's graph keeps what the run wrote whole and cuts only when it serves it, after redaction: a key saved after the graph was read is redacted from a later read too, the one a busy store answers from the kept view included. Each label, error and decision state is redacted once per held key set, not on every poll.
- A workflow run's graph (`GET /v2/runs/{id}/graph`) shows its nodes, decisions and calls (each call's input, output and error) with no held secret in them, by the same rule as the run's answer: a key the server holds or handed the run reads `[redacted]`. Each part is redacted once per content and held key set, so polling a running workflow's graph does not redact it again on every read. A key saved after a read is redacted from the next one.
- A workflow run's graph redacts held keys before it cuts a text, so a cut can no longer leave part of a key: a map item's label (cut at 200 characters), a call's error (cut at 2000, now logged whole up to 64 KB), and a decision's state stored cut past 64 KB (its last characters, where a cut key would sit, are held back).
- A message the person stopped before the harness began it is no longer lost. A Codex or Claude run stopped in its first seconds (Codex still starting), then told "Go on.", answered "I'm missing the earlier task context": the stop withdrew the first message, and the follow-up reached the model alone. Now the stopped message stays in the conversation. The next message carries it first, with a line saying it was stopped before any work on it began, and the stopped turn still never runs on its own. Several stopped messages are carried in order. A stopped steer is not carried. A crash after the next message was taken, before the harness recorded it, still recovers with the stopped words.
- What a run learns is kept item by item. The grader's verdict was read as all or nothing: when it dropped one note and kept another, nothing merged. Now the notes, skill and workflow draft it keeps (or holds for the owner's OK) merge and the ones it drops are left out, and the run's learning names the first one left out. A drop on Planted still keeps nothing from the run, and a grader that keeps every item and says no merge still merges nothing. The checks before the grader work the same way: a note out of shape is left out and the rest goes on (named in the run's learning `problems`), while a secret, a count over its cap, a deletion or a path outside the folder still refuses the whole proposal. And a run's note with no `type:` is a project note, as Claude Code's memory reads one, where it used to drop the whole proposal.
- Learning no longer drops a preference you asked an agent to remember on a server with a Jev key. With `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` set, each new note went through a page-injection classifier before the grader, and a stated preference ("always answer in tables") reads like an instruction to it, so it was dropped as "Not kept: a new note read like instructions planted in a page, not a lesson." The classifier cannot see where a note came from. That screen is gone: the grader's Planted question decides, with what the user said in the run in view. The `screen` and `screen_down` reasons are no longer produced; older runs that hold them still read. One grader now judges every new note, skill and draft: with no `--grade-model` (and no `AGENT_MODEL_STRONG`), that is the agent's own model, the only judge of what it learns. When learning is on, name a strong grade model.
- The end-of-run learning prompt says when a job is a workflow draft rather than a skill (one that runs end to end as model and tool-call steps from a defined input to a defined output, or that the person asks to keep as a workflow), and its example draft is whole and valid: schemas declared once, `input` and `output` naming them by `schemaId`, a chain of a `web_fetch` call (when the agent has it) and a model node. Before, agents asked for a workflow kept a skill, and the drafts they wrote were refused (an inline schema in `input`, or none named), as the only example elided its parts. A test validates the prompt's example with the workflow DSL and the learning checker. The prompt also names the node kinds a learned workflow may use: model and tool-call nodes only.
- A learned workflow's call reads on a run's timeline as the workflow it ran and what it gave: "Ran the learned workflow price-report", then "Ran the learned workflow price-report: total 49.75", its output in one line through the same redaction as any tool's summary. It read "Using tool: learned_workflow" / "ok" before.
- A workflow output's number that equals a held all-digit key in value is withheld as `[redacted]`, even when its text differs from the key's (a leading zero, `1e21`, a long key read lossy). Long runs of a GitHub fine-grained token's start (`github_pat_`) or of a JWT's first part are now redacted in time linear in their length; what is redacted is unchanged.
- A model request under a spend cap is booked by one rule on every harness: the kernel's `bookRequest`, from how the
  request ended (`Ended`). The rule is never to under-count.
  - **A whole answer** books its own cost, or its hold when that cost cannot be read. On pi, that used to make what the run
    had spent unknown.
  - **An error status before any event**, or a request refused at connect, books nothing.
  - **A failure once the request left, a gateway's 504 or 524, or an answer after an attempt that was lost** books the
    hold.
  - **A stop** books what streamed: its content, and the part of a hidden reasoning budget that did not stream.
  - **A content-filter end** on pi books the hold: pi-ai ends it as an error after events, which can have been billed. On
    the proxy, a filtered answer is an answer and books its own cost.

  On pi, the cap now gives pi-ai a fetch that records each attempt pi-ai makes, so a reset before any response books the
  hold where it booked $0, and a 429 then an answer is priced once. An API whose transport that fetch cannot see (Codex's
  WebSocket, Bedrock, Google, Cloudflare) books the hold for a failure before any event. A later process reads the figure
  this one booked: a pi request that failed before any event marks its hold, and the runner writes `bookedUsd` and `rule` on
  its records. The model proxy passes how a request ended as `afterRequest`'s third argument.
  If the store cannot take such a mark within 15 s, the request fails, saying its end could not be recorded, and a later
  process reads it at its hold.
- An OpenCode run the server stopped in its first seconds (a restart, a deploy) now picks up where it was when the server comes back, and answers the person's message. Before, the turn was ended as if the person had stopped it, and the run waited for input with the message unanswered. A person's own Stop still stops the turn for good, and "Go on." is a turn of its own.
- A paid tool's spend-cap refusal (a browser, monid or Parallel call) no longer counts money held for requests still
  answering as spent. It is now one sentence, like a model request's refusal: what the run spent, and what it holds for
  calls still answering, of its cap; what the call could cost; and the cap that would run it, at least a cent above the
  current one. Every amount uses one format. A model request refused for no price, no output ceiling, an unmeasured input
  or an unknown spend now says "not sent" once, and writes its cap in that format too.
- A run create retried with the same `Idempotency-Key` after the server went down mid-create now answers the run the first try made, never a second run. The agent store records the key's origin in the transaction that writes the run: `store.startRun({ agent, origin: { key, fingerprint? } })` starts one run per key and, asked again (or by two starts at once), returns that run with `existing: { fingerprint? }` and writes nothing; `store.runOfOrigin(key)` reads it. The new `run_origins` table comes without a new store version. `POST /v2/agents/{agent_id}/runs` gives the store its key's slot and body, hashed, so a retry after a restart replays the run (`Idempotent-Replayed: true`) and another body under the key is still `409 idempotency_mismatch`; a create's key is kept with its run, past `limits.idempotency_ttl_s`.
  A run the server went down on after the store made it and before it was set up (no input saved in its `serve.json`) used to be relaunched with no input and stay queued for good; the next start now fails it with `error.details.reason` `server_lost_at_setup`, and never launches it.
- A Docker box's network label (`agentrun.net`) is the same for the same hosts in whatever order and case `AGENTRUN_BOX_ALLOW` names them, so reordering the setting no longer makes a run's box again. A box labelled before this with a list that was not in sorted order is made again once, at its run's next open. The serve README no longer says Docker Desktop on WSL2 always passes the runtime check: it keeps its data outside the distro, so its box has no network unless the distro has that directory.
- An OpenCode tool call leaves nothing behind once its client is gone and the run's MCP server has closed. Its progress timer stops, and nothing more is written to its response, the moment its client goes away; the call itself still counts as running until it settles, so a drive's end still waits for its real outcome. When the server closes, a call that is still running after its 5 s window is let go: its timer is stopped, it no longer counts as running, and its answer, if it ever comes, is written nowhere. Before, a host tool that never settled kept a live timer writing progress to a closed response, and stayed counted as running, for as long as the server process lived.
- A spend cap refusal names a raise that runs what it refused. The cent it rounds up to is read within the same float tail the cap's fit check allows, so a total a hair past a whole cent names the cent above it, not that cent.
- Schedules keep firing after a crash or a bad trigger. A trigger's firings are driven one at a time, in order, so a firing a crash left due settles to the run it had before the next start's own firing is checked, which then starts its run instead of being skipped as waiting on it. A trigger whose cron line or zone no longer reads (a newer cron library or time zone data refuses it) is turned off alone with `disabled: {reason: "source_unreadable", detail}`, at a start or when it comes due, and every other schedule goes on firing; the log names it instead of a failed write, and nothing is retried for it. A first start that sends `trigger.failed` takes failures an earlier server recorded as already told. A trigger's input is checked at create and PATCH as its runs are given it, with the line saying what fired it.
- `GET /usage`: a run whose cost is unknown (a model request a restart cut, a paid call a crash cut, a provider with no price) adds what it is known to have spent so far to the total and its lines, and is still counted in `unpriced_runs`. A total is never below a line of its own (a run's browser time no longer drops out of the total), and with `unpriced_runs` above 0 it is a lower bound.
- GET /stats counts a run that has just ended as ended, as GET /runs/{id} already says, never as running or as a run of unknown cost. A run reads ended as soon as its turn ends, and its end, with its metrics, reaches the agent store after its process has let it go. Before, /stats counted it meanwhile as a running run: status unknown, cost unknown, effort unset, no tokens. Now, while its end is on its way, it is counted completed (or failed, or stopped) in the run counts and the totals, and its tokens, cost and effort are left out until the store has them, when it counts whole. `AgentStoreReader.analytics` takes `pending`, the runs whose end the caller holds, by the state their end will have.
- A request you stop under a cap counts the reasoning it never streamed. A model whose reasoning streams only as a summary (the Responses API's reasoning, Claude 4-class thinking) is billed for far more than the summary's bytes. A stop after about 10,000 hidden reasoning tokens on a gpt-5-class model with a 20 KB context cost about $0.106 and counted $0.038, so deliberate stops could pass a cap. A stopped request that reasons now also counts the part of its reasoning budget that did not stream: input + streamed + 1,024 + max(0, reasoning budget - reasoning streamed), never more than its hold. Real usage still replaces the figure when it comes.
  - **On pi:** a reasoning model's stopped request counts its thinking level's budget (on Anthropic's API, the `budget_tokens` it was sent with, for a model whose thinking takes one). A Claude model that thinks adaptively (every current one on Anthropic's API) is sent no budget, and its thinking is bounded only by its output limit, so it counts that limit less 1,024 tokens: in practice its hold. A stop after a 300-byte thinking summary on Sonnet 5.5 with a 20 KB context under a $1 cap counted $0.142 at medium and $0.224 at high, against about $0.45 for 40,000 adaptive thinking tokens. The request's output limit is now the one actually sent: the cap had taken a thinking budget out of it that pi-ai never sends (8,192 tokens at medium), so the request was held at more than it was sent with. With no level, on an API whose model reasons at its own default, it counts its whole output limit. With thinking off on Anthropic's API, on a model whose thinking can be off, nothing more.
  - **Adaptive thinking counts up to the hold.** On every current Claude model, on pi and on `claude-agent`, the provider is given no thinking budget, so a stopped request counts up to the hold the cap took for it. Under a cap with little left beside that hold, the request after the stop can be refused.
  - **On `codex`:** the effort's budget (minimal 1,024, low 2,048, medium 8,192, high 16,384), none at effort `none`, and the output limit when the body names no effort.
  - **On `opencode`:** the same as `codex` (the same Responses API): what a stop counts as streamed is the content its deltas carried, not the wire, plus the unstreamed part of its effort's budget. On the walk through OpenCode's proxy (a request at $100 a million output tokens under a $0.50 cap, stopped after 1,146 characters of answer text after the stream's echo of the request) the stop booked 106,831 bytes, its whole $0.50 hold, and refused "Go on."; it now counts $0.217, and "Go on." goes.
  - **On `claude-agent`:** the thinking budget, or `max_tokens` when thinking is adaptive, for every request that thinks; none with thinking off. Thinking that streamed is subtracted, so a stop after a budget's worth of visible thinking counts nothing on top.
  - **Kernel:** a provider policy may read a request's hidden reasoning from its body (`reasoningHidden`). It may also read the reasoning in each streamed event (`reasoningBytesOf`). The model proxy books a stopped response with both (`stoppedHiddenTokens`, `stoppedReasoningBytes`), and the runner counts all it sent plus the part of the budget its reasoning did not cover.
  - **What streamed is the content, not the wire.** On `claude-agent` and `codex` a stop counted the response's bytes as sent, the provider's framing included: about 11.7 bytes a character of text on the Responses API and 24 on the Messages API. A codex request at $100 a million output under a $0.50 cap, stopped after 1,146 characters, booked its whole hold and refused "Go on.". It now counts each text, reasoning and tool-argument delta's UTF-8 bytes, as pi counts its streamed blocks: $0.217, and the next request goes. A provider policy reads them (`contentBytesOf`).
  - **A stop before the provider's first header** on `claude-agent` and `codex` now counts as a stop with nothing streamed (its input and 1,024 tokens, as on pi), not its whole hold. A response the provider answered with an error status still counts at its hold.
- A message the person stopped the instant it was sent is no longer lost on claude-agent, and the same window is closed on Codex and OpenCode. Asked "Reply with exactly this code and nothing else: CARRY-CLAUDE-7", stopped within its first second, then told "Go on.", a claude-agent run answered "There's nothing earlier in this conversation for me to continue from": Claude takes a message within its first second, so the stop landed after the harness placed it and before the CLI was handed it, and nothing carried it. Now a stop's settle carries a placed message its CLI never took into the next one, as it carries one stopped before it was placed. On claude-agent that is a message the CLI's own transcript, as the session keeps it, does not hold: that transcript is the history a resumed CLI is given, so a carried message is never sent twice. On Codex it is a message Codex's own rollout, as the session keeps it, does not hold. OpenCode marks a message just before it sends it (`prompt_async`), and a marked message is never carried: a crash between the mark and the send loses it rather than sending it twice. After a stop, no new turn is started.
  - **A recovered message is not carried again.** After a crash between a message's placement and its CLI's record, the recovery message carries its words. It now records which messages it carries, before it goes (a kernel effect by its id on claude-agent, its `PROMPT_STREAM` record on OpenCode). Once the CLI holds the recovery message, a stop's settle and a later recovery both count those messages sent, so nothing is sent twice, even after a second crash. Codex's recovery already did this through its client id.
  - **A follow-up's own prompt** (the host's, named to the settle) is withdrawn and never carried into the person's next message, as the follow-up says: a host prompt that never ran is not sent. A person's message waiting beside it is left as it is.
- A Codex or Claude run stopped right after a tool finished, then told "Go on.", showed the follow-up as "You (steer): Go on." in the timeline, as if it had joined the stopped turn. The timeline now reads the turn's end that serve writes, so the follow-up shows as "You: Go on.".
- A person's own message shows as they typed it, with any held key redacted. Its Timeline row (summary and text) shows a pasted key as `[redacted]`, and a pasted URL keeps its fragment and query (a console link's `#/runs/<id>` no longer reads as the bare origin). A run's title, taken from an agentic run's first message or a workflow run's input, holds no held key on the run's page, the runs list, Mission Control or an agent's runs, including a key a long value's cut would have split. The agent still gets its message and input exactly as given: only what is shown changes.
- The webhook ledger test closes its first server once each try has its answer, not once the receiver has the request, so a slow CI runner no longer cuts a try and gets the message twice; the test is out of quarantine.
- The tool gate's `Executor` contract says who bounds an approved call: the executor does. The gate puts no wall-clock bound on it (only a stop or a cancel cuts it, through its `signal`), so an executor bounds its own work and answers a timeout as an error result.
- The serve README's spend cap section says why a stopped request on a Claude model that thinks adaptively counts its whole thinking budget: the provider reports the thinking it billed only at the end of its stream, which a stopped stream never receives, so that budget is the safe figure.

## 0.1.0-beta.29 (2026-11-04)

- New held package `@parcha/agentrun-serve`: the Agentrun v2 API (`agentrun.v2/0`, the contract the hosted API serves) on
  your own machine. `agentrun-serve` serves `http://127.0.0.1:7340/v2` over agent homes of `@parcha/pi-durable-agent` in a
  data directory (default `~/.local/share/agentrun/agents`, `--data`), with a bearer token it writes there at mode 0600 and
  prints at start with `AGENTRUN_BASE_URL`. It binds loopback only, refuses a `Host` that is not a loopback name, and sends
  no CORS headers. It serves capabilities, agents (create, list, read), runs (start, list, read, the timeline as JSON pages
  with the change-number cursor and as server-sent events, messages that steer, answer or follow up, stop, cancel), a
  run's files under `work/`, and usage as an estimate. Each run is a child process of `pi-durable-disk run`, read through
  its door while it is live and from its released store after; a run that answered is carried back to its home and is
  finished. A run's tools are the ones its definition names, of `read`, `write`, `edit` and `ask_person`, which ends the
  turn until a message answers it. With an `output_schema`, a run's output is its final answer parsed as a JSON object,
  and an answer that is not one ends it `failed` (`output_invalid`). `--faux MODULE` answers every model request from a module, with no key and no network; without it the model is
  an OpenAI Responses endpoint named in `AGENT_MODEL_BASE_URL` and `AGENT_MODEL`. Home export and import, definition
  updates, uploads, quotes and kill answer `501 unsupported_here`, and capabilities say so.
- A run that a restarted server adopted now ends final after it answers. The server read the run's store as soon as the run said it was released, while the run's process could still hold the store's exclusive lock until it exited; that read failed with `database is locked`, was not retried, and left the run `completed` but never final until the next start. The server now reads an adopted run's store once its process has exited (it reads anyway, and says so, if the process lives on 30 s after it released the run), retries a store that another connection holds within the same bounded retries as a busy agent home, and writes each wait and a run it could not settle or recover to stderr.
- `packages/serve`: the recovery tests' stand-in run writes `run.json` whole, through a temporary file and a rename, as a run does. A plain rewrite let the server's 500 ms read land on a half-written record, read it as no record, and settle the adopted run early, which failed "an adopted run that released is read only once its process has exited" about once in a loaded full run.
- A half-written JSON file can no longer crash agentrun-serve. Reading a run's `run.json` or a `serve.json` caught a failed read but not a parse error, so a half-written file threw out of the adoption timer as an unhandled rejection, which ends a Node process by default. A file that is not whole JSON now reads as no file. An adopted run whose record cannot be read while its process lives is read again at every tick and stays supervised; the server says so once it has been unreadable for 5 s. The server writes its own `serve.json` files whole, through a temporary file and a rename.
- `agentrun-serve` serves one agent store of `@parcha/pi-durable-agent` instead of an agent home per agent: the data directory holds `store.sqlite` and `runs/<id>/`, and beta.24 agent homes in it are not read. An agent is a row with revisions written once; a definition with `instructions` makes an agentic revision and one with `workflow` (an agentrun-dsl workflow, checked before anything is written) a workflow revision, and an agentic one may name its `model` as `provider/model-id` and its `thinking` level (never off; a run's `effort` outranks it, and `AGENT_THINKING` is the host's default). `PUT /v2/agents/{id}/definition` now commits a new revision whose parent is the current one and makes it current (without `skills`, the current revision's skill files carry over; `If-Match` guards it), `GET /v2/agents/{id}/revisions` lists them, `POST /v2/agents/{id}/current` makes one current again, and `GET /v2/runs` lists every agent's runs, newest first, by agent, revision and status. A run pins the current revision or the one `POST .../runs` names, and the store's row records it; the Agent's ETag is its current revision. A definition's `input_schema`, `output_schema` and `max_cost_usd` have no place in a revision and are refused by name (per-run `max_cost_usd` stays). Capabilities list the `tools` an agentic revision may name and what a workflow's model nodes get (`workflows`).
  A workflow revision's run starts its workflow with `startWorkflowRevision` of `@parcha/pi-durable-agent/workflow` in the run's process, each model node on the host's model of its tier (`AGENT_MODEL_FAST` and `AGENT_MODEL_STRONG`, each falling back to `AGENT_MODEL`, listed in capabilities' `models`), and the server carries it back with `workflowCarry` once the process has exited, as it carries back every run; its output is the run's `result.output`, its rows come when it ends, and it takes no messages; one that escalated is `failed` with `error.code` `escalated` and its summary. A workflow that needs a tool call, a judge or code is not runnable here (`409`), and an input its workflow does not take is `422`. A model comes from `--faux`, from the `AGENT_MODEL_BASE_URL` gateway (every model id through one endpoint), or else from pi-ai's providers with their keys from the environment; `AGENT_MODEL` is the default for a revision that names none, and a model that cannot be reached here is `409 agent_not_runnable`. When the server starts, a run whose process went before the run ended is opened again on its directory and resumes from its store, where it was marked failed.
- `agentrun-serve` runs in one container: `--listen ADDR` listens on another address than 127.0.0.1 (there every `Host` name is served and the token is the only lock), and `AGENTRUN_TOKEN` gives the token in place of `DIR/.token`'s and is never printed. The repository has `packages/serve/docker/Dockerfile` (the server and the open packages it runs, everything it keeps under `/data`), a compose example that publishes the port on loopback only, and `render.yaml`, a Render blueprint with a persistent disk and a generated token. The image's build context is an allowlist (`.dockerignore`): a `.env`, `.env.*` or `.npmrc` never enters it, wherever it is in the checkout.
- `agentrun-serve` serves a page at `/`, AgentRun Server, for the person who holds its token: the agent store's page from `@parcha/pi-durable-agent` (agents, revisions with the prompt diff and the workflow graph, runs, a run's timeline) plus every run across agents, forms that create an agent, commit a revision, make a revision current and start a run through `/v2`, and a live run followed until it is final, with an answer box while it waits on `ask_person`. The server prints one more line, `Open` and the page's address with `?token=` and its token; opening it sets an `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Strict` cookie that stands in for the Bearer header (a write only from the page's own origin with a JSON body).
  `@parcha/pi-durable-agent/serve` exports what another server needs to serve the store's page: `storeApi(store)`, the read-only JSON behind its `/api/*` paths, and `STORE_PAGE_FILES`, its files. The page takes a script loaded before `page.js` that sets `window.agentStoreHost`; `pi-durable-agent serve` loads none. A cost under a cent now shows two significant digits ($0.00014, not $0.000), and a recorded 0 shows as $0.
- `PUT /v2/agents/{id}/definition` with `If-Match` is compared inside the store's commit, so of two edits that read the same revision exactly one lands and the other answers `412`, where both could land, the second on top of the first. A run's process loads only the model tiers its revision uses, so a workflow of fast nodes runs with `AGENT_MODEL_FAST` and no `AGENT_MODEL`. A run's process no longer gets the server's token: `AGENTRUN_TOKEN`, `AGENTRUN_API_KEY` and any variable holding the token are left out of its environment, and model keys and the `AGENT_MODEL` variables still reach it.
- The README's Safety section says what holds a run in: its file tools reach its own `work/`, plus reads of its own `tmp/` and writes in a temporary directory made for a tool, and nothing else (pi-durable-disk's confinement in the run's process, since the run and the server share a user), and no path offers a shell, so `bash` stays off. A new suite of real runs probes it: reads of the server's and the door's tokens, `store.sqlite`, another run's `work/` and `tmp/`, `/proc` and a link planted in `work/`, writes and an edit outside `work/` (its own `tmp/` included), and `bash` called by an agent and by a workflow's model node are each refused, with nothing changed on disk, while the same run still reads and writes inside `work/` and reads its own `tmp/`.
- Through a gateway (`AGENT_MODEL_BASE_URL`), a revision that names its model as `provider/model-id` is now sent to the gateway whole (`openai/gpt-4o`), as a gateway like OpenRouter names its models `vendor/model`; it was sent as `model-id` alone. Who breaks: a gateway that expects the id without its provider. What to do: set `AGENT_MODEL_GATEWAY_IDS=bare` to keep the old behaviour. Unchanged: `AGENT_MODEL`, `AGENT_MODEL_FAST` and `AGENT_MODEL_STRONG` are sent as they are set, and the price is still the named provider's catalog entry for the model, else the highest.
- Archive an agent or a run: `POST /v2/agents/{id}/archive` and `POST /v2/runs/{id}/archive` (and `.../unarchive`), this server's own routes outside the `/v2` contract, listed in `capabilities.features.archive`. An archived agent and its runs, or one archived run, are hidden from `GET /agents`, `GET /runs` and `GET /agents/{id}/runs` (`?archived=true` lists only those), and kept: every read by id still answers, with `archived_at`, and `/usage` still counts them. A new run of an archived agent is `409 agent_not_runnable`; a run already going keeps going. Nothing is deleted, and `DELETE` stays `405`. The page has Archive and Unarchive on an agent's page and a run's page, and Show archived on the agents and on All runs.
- `AGENTRUN_PUBLIC_URL` sets the address the printed `Open` link and `AGENTRUN_BASE_URL` name, so a container published on another port, or behind a proxy, prints a link that works; a value that is not an http(s) URL stops the server at start. The compose example's `AGENTRUN_PORT` publishes another port and sets that URL. `render.yaml` and the compose example take `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` (pi-ai's OpenRouter provider, `AGENT_MODEL=openrouter/<model>`) and a gateway's `AGENT_MODEL_BASE_URL` and `AGENT_MODEL_API_KEY`. The root README opens with AgentRun as an open platform to manage your agents (the platform, AgentRun Server and the building blocks), then the two ways to host it: `docker run` with the public image, or Render's Starter with a disk.
- `GET /v2/openapi.json` serves this API as an OpenAPI 3.1 document, with no credential, as capabilities are, and capabilities link it (`links.openapi`). It is the `agentrun.v2/0` contract, vendored unchanged in `src/openapi/agentrun-v2.openapi.json` with its source (repository, pull request and commit) in `info.x-agentrun-source`. The server marks its own routes outside the contract (`GET /runs`, `GET /agents/{id}/revisions`, `POST /agents/{id}/current`, and the document itself) `x-agentrun-serve: true`, and the six operations it answers `501` (home export and import, kill, the two upload routes and quotes) `x-agentrun-serve-unsupported` with the reason. A test holds the document to the server's route table, both ways.
- **The page is yours: one owner and a password, on by default**
  - AgentRun Server is for one person, who signs in to the page with a password. This is on unless `AGENTRUN_ACCOUNTS=0`. With no `AGENTRUN_PUBLIC_URL` (as `docker run` gives it; on Render, its `RENDER_EXTERNAL_URL` stands in), the first visit sets the password, 12 characters or more, and signs in. With it, setup takes the code the server prints, or the token as the Bearer. A second setup is a 403. `startServe` takes this as `accounts` and `openSetup` (both off by default for a library caller).
  - The password is kept as a scrypt hash (N 2^15, r 8, p 1, with a 16-byte salt and its parameters) in `DIR/owner.json` (0600). A session is a cookie signed with HMAC-SHA256 under the key in `DIR/.session-secret` (0600). It holds when it was made, when it ends (30 days, checked from the signed payload) and the session epoch. The cookie is HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict and Path=/, `__Host-agentrun-session` with Secure on https. No run's tool reaches either file.
  - `POST /v2/setup`, `POST /v2/auth/sign-in`, `POST /v2/auth/sign-out` and `GET /v2/auth/session` take writes only from the page's own origin with a JSON body. Sign-in tries are limited to 10 per address in 15 minutes, keyed on the connection's peer, never on a header. **Sign out everywhere** ends every session on every browser.
  - `agentrun-serve reset-password --data DIR`, run where the data directory is, clears the password and ends every session; the next visit sets a new one. It is never an HTTP route.
  - The token keeps working for scripts and the CLI. With accounts on, a `?token=` page link sets no cookie and the start's `Open` line holds no token: the page is the owner's session alone, so **Sign out everywhere** leaves no browser in. The sign-in limit keeps up to 10,000 addresses; past that a new one gets a 429 and an address already limited stays limited.
  - Every route but capabilities, the contract and health takes the owner's session or the token. The contract's answers do not change.
- On pi-durable 1.2 and later, the model's record of an `ask_person` call was empty: the tool returned its text under `content`, which 1.2 renamed to `output`. It now returns both, so every pi-durable serve takes (1.1 to 1.3) reads it. The released image ships pi-durable 1.1.0 and was not affected; builds from source on 1.2 or 1.3 were.
- AgentRun Server caps its concurrent runs, says whether it can work, and settles runs a full disk left behind.

  - The most runs at once (each with a live process) is `--max-runs N` or `AGENTRUN_MAX_RUNS`, by default `max(1, floor((memory − 128 MiB) / 72 MiB))` from the container's memory limit or the machine's (5 on a 512 MB instance, which ran out of memory at about 6 before). Over it, `POST /v2/agents/{id}/runs` is `429 rate_limited` with `retry-after`, and nothing starts. `capabilities.limits.max_concurrent_runs` reports it.
  - `GET /v2/health`, with no credential, is `200` when the agent store opens for a read and the data directory's disk has 64 MiB free (`AGENTRUN_MIN_FREE_MB`), else `503` naming the check that failed, and no path, size or version. Compose's healthcheck and `render.yaml`'s `healthCheckPath` use it; before, the health check was `/v2/capabilities`, which stayed `200` on a full disk.
  - A run whose end the agent store cannot write (a full disk) still ends: a cancel makes it `cancelled`, priced from its rows, where before it stayed `queued` or `working` and cancel could not settle it. The server writes the end again every 30 s until it lands.
  - A message to a run whose process was killed opens it again at once, where before it was `503` for up to the 90 s lease: a heartbeat of a process that is gone is no longer trusted.
  - A free-disk reading that fails is `disk: "cannot_check"` (`503`), never room. A data directory serves one AgentRun Server at a time; two on a shared disk are not supported.
- Through a gateway (`AGENT_MODEL_BASE_URL`) with no `AGENT_MODEL_PRICE`, a model id pi-ai's catalog knows now takes the catalog's list price, so its runs' costs are estimated instead of reading $0. A revision's `provider/model-id` takes that provider's entry when it is paid; a bare id (`AGENT_MODEL` and the tier variables), a provider the catalog does not list for it, or one it lists at $0 takes the highest price any provider lists: one entry's whole price, the most input plus output, never a $0 entry over a paid one, so a named provider's $0 never switches off `max_cost_usd`. A price you set still wins, and an id the catalog does not know is still priced at $0. A behavior change: a run's `max_cost_usd` now applies to these gateway models and stops the run with `spend_cap_reached` once its estimate passes the cap; at $0 it never fired.
- **A run that ended is never started again, so its place is free once it reads failed**
  - A run's first message waits for the run's process to come up. When that process failed at its start, the settle that ended the run could finish while the wait read the run's `run.json`, and the wait then started the process again: a second process for a finished run, holding its place, so the next start under `--max-runs` could be a 429 `rate_limited`.
  - The wait now checks whether the run ended after each read, and a run that ended or is finishing is never launched.
- A run whose process fails as it starts is no longer started again while its end is being written. The first message's delivery could start a second process in that window, so the run read `failed` while that process still held its place under `--max-runs`, and the next start was refused (429). Nothing starts a run's process now while a settle of that run is in progress.

  A run whose first message was still on its way when the server stopped no longer has its end written after the stop, into a store the stop had closed. The next start recovers the run and writes its end then. A run's first message also waits out a process that is slow to come up, up to the ready time (45 s), whatever deadline a later request to the run has.
- AgentRun Server is easier to run: a stop ends within the platform's grace, the token link takes any token, and stdout says what happened.

  - `--drain-timeout D` sets how long a busy run may finish its turn when the server stops (default 30s), and the image's command sets 6s, so a busy stop ends in about 7.5 s, inside Docker's default 10 s grace, so a busy `docker stop` no longer ends in SIGKILL (exit 137). Compose sets `stop_grace_period: 15s`. Render waits 30 s and does not let a service with a disk change it, which `render.yaml` now says. A turn cut off resumes at the next start.
  - The page's `/?token=` link signs in with a token holding `+`, `/` or `=` whether it is URL-encoded or not (a `+` sent raw arrives as a space), and the printed link encodes the token. Before, Render's generated tokens often had a `+`, and the documented link answered 401.
  - stdout gets one logfmt line per request (method, path without its query, status, ms), per run start and end (run, agent, status, cost) and per refused credential (route and reason, never the token or any part of it). `AGENTRUN_LOG=quiet` leaves out the request lines. A run whose process failed at its start says why in words, not as the process log's raw JSON.
  - `DIR/.token`'s token is printed only by the start that made it; a later start prints `AGENTRUN_API_KEY=<in DIR/.token>` and `?token=<in DIR/.token>`. `startServe` returns `tokenMade`, takes `log`, and `banner` is exported.
- AgentRun Server refuses to start on a data directory that holds agent homes from 0.1.0-beta.26 or earlier (`<data>/<agent>/agent.sqlite`), naming each one, instead of starting with none of their agents or runs showing. That includes a volume already started on 0.1.0-beta.27 that still holds one, an agent named `runs`, and a symlinked home. The serve README's "Upgrading from 0.1.0-beta.26" says what to do: keep the earlier version on that directory, or start this one on a new directory, or with the old homes moved out.
- `POST /v2/agents` and `PUT /v2/agents/{id}/definition` now check a definition for what keeps it from running here, with the checks a run makes before it starts. A definition that can never run here is `422 input_invalid` by field and nothing is saved: a tool this host does not bind (`definition.tools[i]`, `bash` among them), a model pi-ai does not know (`definition.model`), empty instructions (`definition.instructions`), or a workflow that needs a tool call, a judge, code or a node tool other than `read`, `write` and `edit` (`definition.workflow`). Before, these saved (`201`/`200`) and every run was `409`. A definition this host cannot run yet but could (no key for the model's provider, no default model, no model for a workflow tier) still saves, and the answer lists what is missing in `warnings`; the page shows them. Runs of such a revision, or of one saved before these checks, are still `409 agent_not_runnable`.
- `max_cost_usd` is now a cap, checked before each model request in the run's own process: when what the run has spent plus the request's worst case (its input tokens at the model's input price, its max output tokens at the output price) would pass the cap, the request is not sent and the run ends `failed` with `spend_cap_reached`. Before, it was checked only after each response, so one request could cost any amount over the cap. Spent so far is read back from the run's store when its process starts, so it survives a restart. The server's check after each response stays as a backstop, and a run the cap stopped now keeps the answer it paid for in `result.final_answer` instead of `result: null`.

  A cancelled run, and any run that made no model request, now costs $0 instead of `null`, and `/v2/usage` adds a run whose cost is unknown as $0, so one such run no longer makes every total and line `null`. The README says what `limits.max_cost_usd: null` means: no ceiling on the cap a run may name.

  A response of unknown price (one with no cost in its usage) leaves its run's `cost.usd` `null`, never $0, and `/v2/usage` counts such runs in `unpriced_runs`, in its total and in each line, beside the dollars of the runs whose cost is known.
- A run's spend cap now counts every request's input on every harness, with one bound: a token per UTF-8 byte of what is sent, plus what the provider adds server-side. That bound is at the dearer of the model's input and cache-write prices, plus the request's output ceiling. What the provider adds is documented for Anthropic and OpenAI (their tool-use prompt and per-image maximum). For every other API kind it is assumed: 2,048 tokens for a request with tools, and the largest per-image maximum for each image. So the bound never undercounts as documented for Anthropic and OpenAI, and as an assumed bound elsewhere.
  - codex and claude-agent: before, the cap priced only the output ceiling, so a request with a large input could pass a cap it should not. Each request still in flight now holds its own input bound too, so requests sent side by side cannot pass the cap together. A request whose input could not be measured is refused under a cap.
  - pi: the input was pi's estimate, about 3.5 characters a token. It is now the byte bound, which is stricter, so **a capped pi run can now be refused a request it used to be allowed**, most of all for dense text such as non-Latin scripts and emoji.
  - A model serve has no price for, a gateway model the catalog does not list and no price is set for, is refused under a cap. Before, it read $0 and never reached one. A model the catalog lists at $0 is a known $0 and passes, and with no cap nothing is refused.
  - No API kind is refused for being one: a capped run on Gemini, Bedrock, Mistral or a Chat Completions gateway is priced as above.
- Uploads work on the first try. `upload.url` in the answer to `POST /v2/files` is now a path from the server's root, `/v2/files/{file_id}`, where it was `/files/{file_id}` relative to `/v2`: a client that appended it to the `/v2` base (`$AGENTRUN_BASE_URL$url`) now sends it to the server's address instead (`${AGENTRUN_BASE_URL%/v2}$url`), or resolves it against the base as a URL; a doubled `/v2` answers a `404` that says so. The route itself, `PUT /v2/files/{file_id}`, is unchanged.
  - A file is sent in one call: `POST /v2/files?path=<its path>` with its bytes as the body (`curl --data-binary @plan.md`), any type but a multipart form, answered `201` with the file `ready`, its length the body's and its hash its own. `content_type` and `sha256` may go in the query too, and `Idempotency-Key` replays the first file for the same bytes. The contract's two-step JSON reservation and `PUT` are kept.
  - `GET /v2/files` lists the server's uploads, newest first, `GET /v2/files/{file_id}` reads one's record, and `DELETE /v2/files/{file_id}` removes it; a run that copied a deleted file keeps its copy.
  - The page's session may send a file in one call with any content type, still only with the page's own `Origin`: `POST /v2/files` is marked to take bytes, beside `PUT /v2/files/{file_id}`.
- `npx @parcha/agentrun` installs the server it was released with: `@parcha/agentrun-serve` is its one dependency, at the release's exact version, so npx installs the two together and nothing is fetched at run time (no `@latest`). The command passes its arguments through, with its store (`--data ~/.agentrun` unless `--data` names another), and the server's CLI picks what it used to: with no `--port`, `agentrun-serve` takes the first free port from 7340, found by listening, so a port another process takes at the same moment is passed over (`--port N` is still exact and fails at once when taken, `0` any); `--open` opens the console's page in the browser once the server listens (never the banner's sign-in link, so the token stays out of the opener's arguments and the browser's history), except in CI or on Linux with no display (the command passes it unless `--no-open`). The CLI sets no model: with no `AGENT_MODEL`, the server's default is the first provider key it has, set where it starts or saved in the console's Settings → Model keys, so a key saved later moves the default with no restart. The release publishes the command after the server, and `release-preflight` refuses a command whose server pin is not the release version, so every release bumps the command too. This release publishes `@parcha/agentrun` 0.3.4.
- `boxSlot` (`@parcha/agentrun-kernel/host`) runs one session's harness boxes one at a time, whatever the isolation: how a box starts and how it is reaped are its arguments, in the shapes of the kernel's new `Box` (`host/box.ts`: `BoxSpec`, `BoxRecord`, `BoxRecorder`, `BoxedChild`, `Reaped`, `BoxError`).
  - `start` is synchronous. It is refused with `BoxError` `box_busy` while the session's last box is live, being reaped, or was not counted empty. The box's start writes its record before the spawn (`ahead`) and before its first process is let go (`started`).
  - The box is reaped as soon as its process exits or is never let go. Only the reap's own count frees the slot. The box's exit status and output are never read, since anything in the box can forge them. A box left with processes, or one nothing could count, rejects with `box_not_empty` and keeps the session from starting another. `adopt` reaps the record a session's last box left before anything starts.
  - serve's `writeDurableSync` and `writeDurable` sync the new file and its directory before they return. `boxRecorder` writes a run's `box.json` with them, and refuses a record that names no writer, since such a record could never be cleared safely.
  - A box that exits while its `started` record is still being written is reaped once that write lands, by the record it wrote. A box that has ended records nothing more, so a late write never replaces the next box's record.
  - The durable writes write every byte or fail. A short write (a disk nearly full) is never synced and renamed as a whole record; the recorder refuses it with `box_record_failed` and leaves no part behind.
- A `claude-agent` run loads the revision's skills through the Claude Agent CLI's Skill tool instead of reading them all in its system prompt. The model is shown each skill as `agentrun:<name>`, loads its `SKILL.md` when it needs it, and reads the skill's other files with `read_skill_file`; the system prompt carries the instructions alone. A skill the adapter does not take (a name that is not lowercase letters, digits and dashes, a `SKILL.md` without front matter giving its `name` and a `description`, a file path that is not plain or is deeper than five segments) stays inlined in the system prompt as before, and so do all of them when together they hold more than 1 MiB. The check is the adapter's own `skillProblems`, so a run is never refused for a skill serve took. `pi` and `codex` runs still inline every skill.
- An agent's page in the console runs the agent and shows its analytics. A run bar under the header takes an agentic agent's first message, or a workflow agent's input as JSON (prefilled with its example, else its fields empty), and an effort from the server's `features.efforts` ("default" sends none). A started run opens on its page; a refusal (a `409 agent_not_runnable`, a `422` with its fields) is shown in the bar, and sending again is the same request under the same `Idempotency-Key`. An archived agent has no run bar. The Runs tab shows each run's effort, and an Analytics tab, on a server whose `GET /v2/stats` answers `analytics`, shows the agent's tokens, time, success, cost, success by harness and effort, top tools, and runs and tokens per day over the last 7 or 30 UTC days or every run, drawn with Mission Control's analytics components: a token sum says how many runs it holds, unmetered requests are named, and a cost not known is never $0.
  - serve's runs index (`GET /v2/runs`, `GET /v2/agents/{id}/runs`) now carries a run's `effort` as `GET /v2/runs/{id}` does: optional, absent for a run started with none, marked `effort/1` in the served OpenAPI document. The document also lists every other field a run answer carries beyond the contract, marked `serve/1`: `revision`, `kind` and `archived_at` on `Run` and `RunSummary`, and `error` on `RunSummary`. Before, a run or a runs-index answer did not meet the document's closed schemas.
- `GET /v2/capabilities` names each harness's default model: `harnesses[].default_model`, the model this server runs for that harness when a definition names none (`provider/model-id`), or null where it runs none (the harness does not run here, or no default model here runs on it; claude-agent never takes a gateway's model). It is the model a save and a run resolve, through one function (`harnessModel`, which `blockersOf` now calls too), so capabilities and saves cannot disagree. The console reads it: the editor's Model field and the agent page offer each harness the default the server names for it, so a codex agent behind a gateway is offered the gateway's default, and a claude-agent agent is told to choose a model. A server that does not name them keeps the console's earlier rule, pi alone. Capabilities also name the routing prefix this server puts before a gateway's model ids, `models.route_prefix` (`agent-model`; null with no gateway), and the console takes exactly that off a model's label, never a first segment read off the id, so a gateway id with no routing prefix keeps its provider. The exported `capabilities()` helper resolves each harness's offer itself and returns a promise of the document the route serves, `default_model` included, so a direct caller's document passes the published schema. The published document's `Capabilities.models.source` takes on `gateway` and `providers` (marked `models/2`): it held only the contract's `managed`, `chatgpt`, `env` and `faux`, so every answer of a server without `--faux` failed the schema the server publishes.
- **A decision Jev answers is booked at TypeSafe's published price, as an estimate**
  - `jevProvider` prices the counted tokens of a TypeSafe call at `JEV_PUBLISHED_PRICING` ($0.042 per million input tokens, output tokens free) unless `pricing` says otherwise, and books it with `cost_basis: "estimate"`. A cost the response reports still wins, booked `reported`. With no token counts, on the OpenRouter route, or for a call that may have been billed, the cost stays `null`, never 0.
  - agentrun-serve: a decision workflow run's `cost.usd` is its decisions' estimated sum, where it was `null` before.
  - agentrun-serve: a run's usage row keeps a decision's cost to the millionth of a cent, so it agrees with the run's `cost.usd`, and a cost above zero but under a hundredth of a cent reads `<$0.0001`, never `$0.0000`.
- OpenRouter's key alone now runs every harness. With `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` set and none of a harness's own settings, claude-agent uses OpenRouter's Messages endpoint and codex its Responses API, both at `https://openrouter.ai/api` with the key as a bearer token. The OpenRouter model key's defaults are `openrouter/openai/gpt-6-sol` for pi, `anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5` for claude-agent and `openai/gpt-6-sol` for codex. Anthropic's and OpenAI's keys still give the default first where they are set. A harness's own settings still win: the endpoint the host names (`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `AGENT_MODEL_BASE_URL`), then the vendor's key. An endpoint the host names never gets the OpenRouter key.
  - claude-agent sends Anthropic's own model id (`claude-sonnet-5-5`) to OpenRouter, as it does direct, and its run is priced by OpenRouter's catalog entry. `claudeProvider` returns `{ origin, credential, auth, modelId, priceModel }` (the `ClaudeProvider` type), so anything else that calls the run's model reads the same answer.
  - A model named `openrouter/<vendor>/<model>` (pi's name for it) runs on claude-agent and codex too. claude-agent used to refuse it as more than one segment.
  - pi now takes serve's gateway at `<base>/v1/responses`, after one trailing `/v1` is taken off the base, as codex and claude-agent do. `AGENT_MODEL_BASE_URL=https://openrouter.ai/api` used to send pi to `/api/responses`, a 404 at OpenRouter.
  - With no model here, the words name `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` first, and claude-agent's name it too.
  - A provider's 401 now fails the run with the key by name and where to set it, on every harness: "OpenRouter refused OPENROUTER_API_KEY (401: User not found): set it where AgentRun starts, or save it in Settings → Model keys." Only the provider's first sentence is kept, and never the key. Anthropic's and OpenAI's keys, and serve's gateway key (`AGENT_MODEL_API_KEY`), read the same way.
- The server admits runs by the memory their harness takes, not by a count that assumed every run costs the same. Each run with a live process reserves its harness's measured size (pi 128 MiB, codex 192 MiB, claude-agent 288 MiB) out of the container's memory limit, or the machine's, less the server's own 128 MiB. A 512 MB instance holds 3 pi runs, 2 codex runs or 1 claude-agent run at once; before, it admitted 5 runs of any harness, and one claude-agent run there killed the container, server included.
  - A start that doesn't fit in what is left is the same `429 rate_limited` with `retry-after` as before, its message naming the harness and the memory it needs and has free (`details.harness`, `needs_mb`, `free_mb`). There is still no queue, and a message to a run already going is never refused for a place. A run's process the next server adopts after a restart holds its harness's memory there; a run whose harness never fits that server fails with why instead of starting again.
  - A harness whose single run can never fit (claude-agent on a host under 416 MB) is unavailable: its capabilities entry says why, a save warns, and its runs are `409 agent_not_runnable` with that reason, not a 429 to retry. pi always has one place.
  - `--max-runs N` and `AGENTRUN_MAX_RUNS` still cap the count, and both limits apply. By default the count is as many pi runs as fit.
  - Capabilities' `limits.max_concurrent_runs` is pi's count (the smallest run, so the most at once). Each `harnesses` entry adds `run_memory_mib` and its own `max_concurrent_runs`, marked `harness/2` in the served document.
- Each agent now has a home, a directory of its own under the data directory that stays across its runs, named by its id in lower case and a hash of the id so ids that differ only in case never share one. `GET /v2/agents/{id}/home/files` lists its files in the shape of a run's file list, and `GET`, `PUT` and `DELETE` on a file's path below it read, write and remove one, whole and never through a link, up to `capabilities.home.max_file_bytes`. `learning/` is the server's: the list shows it as one entry with `host_owned: true` and none of its notes, and a read, write or delete at or under it, in any case, is `403 system_file_forbidden`, since its notes are read through `/v2/agents/{id}/learning`. Where the server has no `/proc/self/fd` to hold a write to its directory (macOS), `capabilities.home.writes` is `false` and a home's `PUT` and `DELETE` answer `501`. Runs do not see the home yet, and `GET`/`PUT /agents/{id}/home` still answer `501`.
- An agent definition can name its place: `{mode: "runner"}`, which has no shell, or `{mode: "sandbox", box}` with `bwrap`, `docker`, `modal` or `daytona`, where the agent's file and shell tools run in the box while its loop, its log and every key stay on the server; any other shape is `422 input_invalid` on `definition.place`. The revision keeps the place it names, an explicit runner included, and a definition that names none keeps none, so existing revisions keep their ids and answers show no place for them. An edit that names no place keeps the agent's. `GET /v2/capabilities` lists `places` (the modes, the default `runner`, and each box with whether this server has it); no box is set up on any server yet, so a sandbox place saves with a warning and its runs are `409 agent_not_runnable` with `details.box`.
- In a Docker sandbox place on Linux, `read`, `write` and `edit` run on this server, on the `work/` the container binds, instead of as a `docker exec` each (half a second or more per call). `bash` still runs in the container, and a run that never calls it gets no container.
  - The file tools take nothing a command in the box can plant. A link anywhere on the way is refused, one pointing inside `work/` included, and so is a file with another hard link. Every directory is walked on held handles (`/proc/self/fd`), so one swapped for a link between the check and the use is refused too. A write goes through a temporary file renamed over the old one, keeping its mode; a new file is `0644`. A refusal names the box's path and says that `bash`, in the box, can still reach the file.
  - A run whose earlier process left its box (a crash mid-`bash`) has that box's leftover commands killed before its first `read`, `write` or `edit`, so none of them changes `work/` under the file tools. A fresh run still makes no box for them.
  - The model still names and sees the box's paths (`/run/agentrun/run/work/...`), mapped one for one onto the bind's source.
  - Where the server reaches directories by path (no `/proc/self/fd`, as on macOS), or the box binds nothing, all four tools run in the box as before.
- The server sets up the boxes `AGENTRUN_BOXES` lists and probes each once at start, all at once and each within 5 s. The boxes are `docker`, `daytona` and the VM provider's, and `bwrap` comes later. Every box is available or not, with a short reason that holds no key, and one `event=boxes` log line says which. A box the list does not name is never probed, and a name that is no box refuses the start. No run uses a box yet. The boxes come from `@parcha/pi-transport`, now an optional peer, and the VM provider's SDK is the host's to install.
  - Fixed: a box key set in the server's environment reached every run's process. The launcher now holds these keys with its other secrets (`DAYTONA_API_KEY`, `MODAL_TOKEN_ID`, `MODAL_TOKEN_SECRET`), and no run's process is handed one, in its environment or on its pipe.
- A run's Docker box has CPU, memory and process limits by default: 2 CPUs' time, 4 GiB of memory with swap included, and 1,024 processes and threads (`--cpu-period 100000 --cpu-quota 200000 --memory 4g --memory-swap 4g --pids-limit 1024`). Before, `docker run` set none, so one run's fork bomb or runaway build could starve the host. `AGENTRUN_BOX_CPUS` (at least `0.01`), `AGENTRUN_BOX_MEMORY` (a size with its unit, such as `8g`, at least `6m`) and `AGENTRUN_BOX_PIDS` (at most 4,194,304) change them. Any other value refuses the start and names the setting. The CPU limit is a CFS quota, the cap `--cpus` sets, so it holds whatever CPU count Docker's VM has, and Docker ignores it with a warning where the kernel gives it no CPU controller. `--cpus` fails in both cases.
  - `dockerBoxes` takes `cpus`, `memory` (bytes, or Docker's size such as `4g`) and `pidsLimit`. They become `--cpu-period 100000 --cpu-quota <cpus × 100000>`, `--memory` with `--memory-swap` the same, and `--pids-limit`. A spec's own `cpus` or `memoryMb` takes the place of the option's, and a spec's `cpus` is now a quota too, where it was `--cpus`. A spec's `memoryMb` now bounds swap too, and `facts()` reports the limits a box got. A value under 0.01 CPUs, a memory that is not positive or not a size, or a `pidsLimit` outside 1 to 4194304 throws when the provider is made.
- With a box host given (`startServe({ box })`: the box's reap and, on a shared server, its run cgroups), serve lets go the boxes runs leave.
  - A run's box (its `box.json`) is reaped once the run process that recorded it is gone, by pid and start time, at start and when the run or its agent is archived. Only the reap's own count lets it go: empty, the record is marked reaped and its cgroup removed.
  - A box a reap cannot empty, or whose reap fails, is quarantined in `box-quarantine.json` with its record kept. Every later start tries it again, by its own record, even after its run has opened another box. A run's boxes share its cgroup, so the cgroup is removed only when no box still held names it.
  - A record with no pid and no cgroup (a crash before the box's first process was known) is cleared only once its writer is gone.
  - Run cgroups that no record names are reaped through the cgroup and removed.
  - No run starts or opens again before the start sweep is done.
  - A start sweep that fails is tried again by the next start, open or relaunch rather than kept, so one transient error never holds every run back until a restart. A cgroup the reap already removed counts as removed.
  - Each durable write of a box record goes through its own temporary file, so two settlements of one run's box at once (a run's archive and its agent's) never share or rename away each other's.
- An agent whose place is `{"mode": "sandbox", "box": "docker"}` runs its `read`, `write`, `edit` and `bash` in a Docker container, on pi, claude-agent and codex alike, while its loop, its log, its model calls and every key stay on the server. The container is made on the run's first such call and labelled with the run's id. It binds the run's `work/` alone at `/run/agentrun/run/work` (a mount source through a link is refused), has no network, gets `LANG` and `HOME` and nothing of the server's environment, and runs as the server's user. It is found again by its label after a crash, the commands the dead process left killed first, and destroyed when the run ends, when it is archived, and by the next start once its run has ended. `bash` takes pi's arguments, with a 300 s default timeout and its output's tail. `read`, `write` and `edit` take a path in the box's work directory only, and their errors name the box's paths, never the server's. This carries the coding-tool source that was proposed on its own (no shell on this machine). `bash` is refused on a runner place, and so is a sandbox place on a workflow, and `browser` in a sandbox place. What the server files in a run's `work/` (monid's long answers, the browser's evidence) is written through held directory handles and never follows a link a box's command planted there. On a host without `/proc/self/fd` (macOS), where a directory is reached by its path, the server files none of these in a sandbox run's `work/`: monid's long answers there say they could not be saved, and the browser's evidence is not filed. A file tool's absolute path under the run's own work directory on this machine names the same file in the box. `AGENTRUN_DOCKER_IMAGE` names the image (default `debian:bookworm-slim`). The console leaves `bash` unticked on a new agent and offers it only on an agent whose place is a sandbox. In pi-transport, `dockerBoxes({ user })` runs a box's programs as that uid:gid, and `endLeftCommands(box)`, from `@parcha/pi-transport/hands`, kills the command groups a gone host left in a box, leaving a tombstone for one not written down yet.
- A revision may name `browser`: a Browserbase browser on `@parcha/pi-browser`'s durable adapter (`snapshot`, `run`, `screenshot`, `browser_read`, `browser_release`, `browser_relaunch`, and downloads and uploads in the run's `work/`), for an agent and for a workflow's model node. It needs `BROWSERBASE_API_KEY` and `BROWSERBASE_PROJECT_ID` in the server's environment, which reach the run's process; without them capabilities list it unavailable with the missing names, and a run of a revision naming it is `409 agent_not_runnable`. A session's custody stays in the run's own store, each page read is filed under `work/evidence/` in a file of its own, parallel nodes' reads included, there is no recording or live view, and the sessions a run still holds are released when the run is let go. agentrun-serve now depends on `@parcha/pi-browser` and the `@browserbasehq/sdk` it drives Browserbase with.
- A spend cap below one request's worst case at its model's output ceiling no longer refuses the run before it starts. Each request is limited to the output tokens what is left of the cap can buy, thinking included, and held at that, so the cap stays exact. A request is refused only when what is left cannot buy its least: 1,024 tokens for its answer, plus its thinking's least when it thinks. A $0.50 cap on Claude Sonnet now runs; before, any cap under about $1.30 could never send a request.
  - A refusal says, in plain words, what the run spent of its cap, what its next request needs and the cap that would run it ("Not sent: ... Raise the cap to at least $0.48 to run it."), on the run's error and its timeline row, after the code `spend_cap_reached (out of budget): `, which pi-ai's retry takes as final. A request the cap refused counts as no model request in the run's usage, on every harness; a run whose only request was refused says "no model request sent". A reply the limit cut short says so on the run's timeline.
  - The model proxy asks the host with a request's own output limit and its least (`outputTokensAsked`, `outputTokensLeast`), and writes the admitted limit (the ticket's `outputTokensMax`) into its body through a policy's new `limitOutput`; it never sends a request unlimited that was admitted with a limit, nor a body longer than the one its input was measured on. The runner's `beforeRequest` may answer `outputTokensMax` beside `holdUsd`, and holds each request in flight at the limit it was admitted with; a limit that is no whole number of tokens above 0 refuses the request, never sends it unlimited.
- The server answers its console (`apps/console`) at `/console/` on its own origin, with the page's CSP and security headers; `/console` redirects there. The console's files are public, like the page's, and its `/v2` calls hold the owner's session the same way. The page at `/` is unchanged.
  - The server's build copies `apps/console/dist` (its `index.html` and hashed `assets/`, never a source map) into `dist/console` when the console was built first (`npm ci && npm run build` in `apps/console`). It refuses an `index.html` with an inline script or style, which the CSP would block, or an absolute `src` or `href`. A server built without the console answers `/console/` with a 404 that says so.
  - `index.html` is `no-store`; the hashed assets are cached for a year. A path with a dot segment, a percent-encoding or a hidden file, a link out of the console's directory, or a kind of file Vite does not build is a 404.
  - The image builds the console into the server's dist and keeps none of its sources or dependencies. `startServe({ console })` points the server at another built console.
- `/` is the console (`apps/console`), and the server's own page is retired: its HTML, `serve.js`, `serve.css`, its setup and sign-in pages, and the store page's files it loaded (`/page.css`, `/page.js`, `/graph.js`). The store's read-only JSON under `/api/*` keeps answering as before, for the token or the browser's cookie.
  - The console's assets are under `/assets/`, with the same CSP and headers it had at `/console/`. `/console/`, and the old `/setup` and `/sign-in` that the printed setup link still names, send the browser to `/` with a 303, and a link's `#code` stays with it. The console runs its own setup and sign-in.
  - Without accounts, the printed `/?token=` link still sets the browser's cookie once and sends it to `/`. Without that cookie, `/` is the console, which says to open the link; a wrong token answers 401 and sets nothing.
  - A server built without the console answers `/` with a 404 that says so.
- A run whose cost isn't fully known now says what it cost so far and how many charges have no price, instead of a bare "unknown". This covers a stop, a cancel, a request a crash cut, a cost still pending, a model with no price, or a paid tool charge no one knows. `cost.usd` stays null, so nothing reads a partial sum as the cost. Beside it, `so_far_usd` is the sum of every charge whose cost is known, and `unpriced` counts the charges that have none: model requests, a workflow's decisions, and paid tool calls. Both are present only while `usd` is null, on the run, the run list and the timeline's usage row (`x-agentrun-serve: "costsofar/1"`). No ceiling is claimed. The usage row's summary reads "Cost so far: $0.0123 known, 1 not priced, over 7 model requests".
  - A run that ended with its cost unknown keeps both figures in its meta file, so its list row says them after the server no longer holds it. The agent store is unchanged, and so are `/v2/usage` and `/v2/stats`.
  - A claude-agent or codex turn stopped while a request was out reads that request as a charge with no price while the run's process is still there, as a settled read does. Live and settled reads agree after a stop.
- An agent whose place is `{"mode": "sandbox", "box": "daytona"}` runs its `read`, `write`, `edit` and `bash` in a Daytona sandbox, on pi, claude-agent and codex alike, as a Docker place does. The server holds Daytona's key (`DAYTONA_API_KEY`, with `AGENTRUN_BOXES=daytona`) and gives it to nothing else: it makes the run's sandbox on the run's first such call, with its network blocked (`networkBlockAll`; a sandbox Daytona does not report blocked is deleted and the call fails), the fixed environment and the run's labels, and the run's process reaches that sandbox only through the server's box relay on loopback, which takes a run's call only with that run's own door token, adds the key to the calls a run's tools make of their own sandbox (a read of it, and its toolbox's programs and files), refuses every other call, and never follows a redirect (nor does pi-transport's Daytona client): a redirect would carry the key. Each run's door now takes a token of its own, `runs/<id>/door-token`, made at its first launch, and a run's end-of-run notes that hold it are refused like any other secret. A sandbox cannot bind the run's `work/`, so the server copies `work/` into it at `/run/agentrun/run/work` when it makes it, copies what changed back at each turn's end and once more before the run's end is recorded (with pi-transport's safe extractor: nothing outside `work/`, no link, a file the server changed meanwhile kept), and destroys it only once that last copy is whole: a copy that fails is tried again before the end is recorded, and one that stops for good (an archive the server cannot read, a limit it cannot pass) keeps the sandbox, said in the log, its auto-delete turned off and set to stop after 15 idle minutes (its disk kept, its compute stopped), woken and copied back by the next start. A run's sandbox that Daytona stopped, paused or archived (or is stopping, pausing or archiving) is started again, an archived one restored, when the server needs it, never replaced. A read of the run's files waits for a copy its turn's end began, and a start pulls a run it finds waiting. What the server files in `work/` meanwhile (monid's long answers) is copied into the sandbox too, and an edit the sandbox makes to such a file comes back. A start pulls, then destroys, the sandbox of a run that ended while its sandbox was left. In pi-transport, a Daytona box takes `network: "none"` (`networkBlockAll`, and `facts().network` is `none` only when Daytona reports it), a workspace the sandbox's user may not create is made with `sudo -n`, and a paused sandbox counts as not running (a host starts it again).
- A workflow run's timeline shows its decisions. Each decision its `judge`, `pick`, `sift`, `route`, `ask` and `verify` nodes recorded is a row of kind `decision` once the run has ended:
  - its summary names the node and the provider that answered, then each answer with the probability of what it said (`coi (jev): is_coi yes 0.92`), or for a route the branch it took (`route: review`, with `unsure` or `refused` when that is why);
  - its text lists the sources the provider cited (title, then URL) and the questions it refused;
  - its `usage` carries its own cost, and the run's usage row adds it in once: one unknown cost makes the total unknown, never a lower figure.
  `TimelineKind` gains `"decision"`.
- AgentRun Server runs decision workflows on Jev. A workflow's typed questions (its `judge`, `pick`, `sift`, `route` and `ask` nodes) run on TypeSafe's System One through `@parcha/agentrun-jev` when the server's environment sets `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` (or `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, for Jev through OpenRouter). Until now, such a workflow was refused at save.
  - Each question's receipt is a row of the run's store before its answer acts. Jev reports no cost, so a receipt keeps its cost unknown, and so does the run: its `cost.usd` reads `null`, never `0`, while a decision's cost is unknown, and still after the server restarts. An ended run the store kept no cost for now reads unknown too.
  - With no key, such a workflow saves with a `not_set_up_here` warning naming the variable. A run of it is `409 agent_not_runnable` with `details.adapters: ["runJudge"]` and `details.decisions: {jev: {reason: "not_set_up_here", needs: ["TYPESAFE_API_KEY"]}}`, which `/v2/openapi.json` declares among `agent_not_runnable`'s details.
  - `GET /capabilities` lists the decision providers in `workflows.decisions.providers` (`[{id: "jev", available, reason?}]`), marked as the server's own in `/v2/openapi.json`. `runJudge` is among `workflows.adapters` once Jev runs.
  - The TypeSafe key never enters a run's environment, which its tools and its model's provider read. The server drops `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` from every run's process and hands its value to a workflow run's process once, on an inherited pipe, for the judge's runner alone. The pipe is the first descriptor after the run's stdio and its secrets pipe, so a server on macOS, which aborts the parent of a spawn with a pipe at descriptor 16 or above, starts and resumes such runs.
