- AgentRun Server runs decision workflows on Parallel Decisions too. A decision node that says `provider: "parallel"` is answered through `@parcha/agentrun-decisions` when the server's environment sets `PARALLEL_API_KEY`; a node that names no provider is still answered by Jev.
  - In `/v2/openapi.json`, `Capabilities.workflows.decisions` is now marked `decisions/2`, and `info.x-agentrun-serve.fields` lists it. A client that validated against `decisions/1` would refuse the new provider id.
  - `GET /capabilities` lists `parallel` in `workflows.decisions.providers`, `available` where its key is set and with a `reason` naming `PARALLEL_API_KEY` where it is not. `runJudge` is among `workflows.adapters` once either provider runs.
  - With no Parallel key, a workflow whose nodes name `parallel` saves with a `not_set_up_here` warning naming the variable. A run of it is `409 agent_not_runnable` with `details.decisions: {parallel: {reason: "not_set_up_here", needs: ["PARALLEL_API_KEY"]}}`. A node naming a provider the server does not have is refused at save, `422`.
  - The Parallel key never enters a run's environment. `PARALLEL_API_KEY` is on the launcher's list of secrets, so the server holds it out of its own environment and never hands it with a run's secrets. The server drops it from every run's process, and hands it, with Jev's key, to a workflow run's process once on the decision descriptor, now as one object of variable name to value, for its decision providers alone.
  - A run's typed questions go through the DSL's `judgeFromProviders`, to the provider each node names: a node naming one this server does not run is refused before its run, never answered by Jev in its place. Each receipt names the provider that answered.
- A server with the Docker box makes its image ready at its start, so a sandbox run's first tool call is never a silent pull. The default image is the server's own, `agentrun-box`: python 3.12 (`python:3.12-slim`, pinned by its digest) with git, curl, node and rg (Debian's `nodejs` and `ripgrep`), about 300 MB, built from a recipe in the package on first use, tagged and labelled (`agentrun.box-image`) with the recipe's hash, and built again only when the recipe changes. `AGENTRUN_DOCKER_IMAGE` names another image instead, which the server pulls when Docker has none. The build or pull runs once, in the background, for at most 10 minutes. The build reaches the network; a box never does.
  - Each box in `GET /v2/capabilities`' `places.boxes` has a `state`: `ready`, `preparing` (available while the image is built or pulled, its `reason` `building the box image (python, git, curl, node, rg)` or `pulling <image>`) or `unavailable`, with the reason.
  - A sandbox run whose first box comes while the image is coming waits for it, with one timeline row (`Preparing the box image: building it (python, git, curl, node, rg)…`, or `pulling <image>…`), then goes on. Its box is made once the image is in.
  - A build or pull that fails, or takes too long, makes the box unavailable with a reason that names the kind of failure and the next step, and holds none of Docker's output. A run that was waiting fails its call with that reason, and the next sandbox run is `409 agent_not_runnable`. A restart tries again.
  - The log has `event=box_image` lines: the image's state at the start, and again when a build or pull ends, with how long it took.
  - `@parcha/pi-durable-agent/timeline` exports `HOST_PHASE` (`agentrun.phase`): an entry a host writes with a `summary` shows as one `phase` row with it, no request and no cost.
- **Workflows on AgentRun Server read documents, call one completion, judge images, and run code nodes when allowed**
  - `pdf_pages`, a workflow call tool: `{ path, maxPages?, dpi? }` draws a document's first pages as PNGs under the run's `work/pages/<stem>/<dpi>/` (long side at most 1568 px, Jev's largest) with its text. A PNG or JPEG passes through, scaled down if larger. `path` is confined to `--documents DIR` (or `AGENTRUN_SERVE_DOCUMENTS`) and the run's `work/`. Needs poppler's `pdftoppm` and `pdftotext`.
  - `llm`, a workflow call tool: one completion on the run's model of the call's tier, no tools and no loop: `{ text, model, usage, cost_usd }`, the cost null when the model has no price.
  - Both are call-only: an agent or a model node naming them is refused (`422`), and `GET /capabilities` marks them `call_only`.
  - Decision nodes' `images` are loaded from the run's `work/`. Nodes naming `provider: "openrouter"` run on OpenRouter's Decisions models, on the OpenRouter key Jev's OpenRouter route reads (`OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, else the gateway's `AGENT_MODEL_API_KEY` on openrouter.ai), handed on the decision descriptor; capabilities list `openrouter` among the providers (under `faux`, startServe's `fauxOpenrouter` fakes it).
  - A call tool that fails on its own fails that item in a collecting `map` instead of the run.
  - `code` nodes run only on a server started with `--allow-code-nodes` (or `AGENTRUN_SERVE_CODE_NODES=1`): they run in the run's process through `new Function`, which is no sandbox. Off (the default), such a workflow saves with a `not_set_up_here` warning and its runs are `409 agent_not_runnable` with `details.code: true`. Calls via a shell or the executor stay refused.
  - Pi's decision rows keep a receipt's images (by digest), the state the judge saw and its cost basis, and a replay returns them.
- A run's `effort` now also adds a short note on how to work, right after the agent's instructions, on every harness (pi, claude-agent and codex), as well as setting the thinking level as before. `low` says to answer directly, search once only for a fact it lacks, and stop; `medium` to plan briefly, check key claims against a second source, and check the answer once; `high` to plan each part, fill the gaps and review the result against the request. On claude-agent the note is system prompt text, not a skill. A run with no `effort` gets no note, and its prompt is unchanged. The notes are one table in `src/effort-guidance.ts`, and the contract's `Effort` description says so.
- A revision may name `fetch_page`: one web page from a public host, `{url}` in and a Page out, `{ok, url, status, title, text, links, refused?, error?}` (the text cut at 60,000 characters, at most 200 absolute http(s) links). An agent gets the Page as JSON text, and a workflow's `call` node `via: "tool", tool: "fetch_page"` runs it and gets the Page as its output: workflows now get a tool adapter beside their model nodes, for the table's tools that a call may run, and a call to any other tool is `409`. It needs no key. It reaches public hosts only, on pi-browser's guard: each redirect hop resolved, checked and dialled at the address checked, http(s) only, at most 5 MiB and 30 s. A loopback, private, link-local, carrier-grade or metadata address, a local name, a public name that resolves to one, a redirect to one, a URL on a port other than 80 or 443, and `file:`, `data:` or `ftp:` are refused as a Page with `ok: false` and `refused`, never a thrown error, so a workflow can branch on it. Capabilities show `calls` for each tool.
- `AGENT_MODEL_BASE_URL` (codex) and `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` (claude-agent) now take a gateway under a path, so one OpenRouter key can serve every harness: `https://openrouter.ai/api` for claude-agent, and `https://openrouter.ai/api/v1` or `https://openrouter.ai/api` for codex and pi. codex sends to `<base>/v1/responses` and claude-agent to `<base>/v1/messages`, with a trailing `/v1` on the base taken once. A base that could send a key elsewhere than it reads is refused before any request, naming its setting: one with userinfo, a query or fragment, a `.` or `..` path segment (raw or encoded), a backslash, whitespace, or plain http to anything but this machine.
- **A definition names its harness: pi, claude-agent or codex**
  - `definition.harness` is `pi` (the default, and what a definition that names none gets), `claude-agent` or `codex`; any other is `422 input_invalid` on the field. Every answer names it. An edit that names no harness keeps the agent's (as it keeps its skill files), and the page sends back the harness it read. The default is never stored, so a revision written before this field and one that names pi are the same revision.
  - `GET /v2/capabilities` lists `harnesses`: each with `available` and, when not, its `reason`. A definition naming a harness this server does not run yet saves with a warning on `definition.harness`, and its runs are `409 agent_not_runnable` with `details.harness`, never run on pi instead. This server runs pi today.
  - `GET /v2/openapi.json` marks every property this server adds to the contract's objects `x-agentrun-serve` with the addition that made it (`harness/1`, and `model/1` and `serve/1` for what it added before), and lists them in `info.x-agentrun-serve.fields`. Without them, the contract is as vendored.
  - The agent store (`@parcha/pi-durable-agent`) keeps an optional `harness` id in a revision's body, so in its id.
- **Runs on claude-agent and codex, durable in the run's own store**
  - An agentic revision whose `harness` is `claude-agent` or `codex` runs on that harness's adapter in the run's own process, behind the run's door: a message starts a turn, a message during a turn waits for its end, a stop interrupts. The session is kept in the run's store, so a run whose process dies mid-turn resumes on the next one.
  - Every paid request is booked once by the adapters' runner booking and shows in the timeline as one model request with its cost, as for pi; a request cut by a crash, or one whose price is unknown, makes the run's cost unknown, never 0. A run opened again reads the cost of all its openings, unknown included.
  - Such a run gets pi's own `read`, `write` and `edit`, with pi's confinement to the run's workspace; `read` may run again after a crash, `write` and `edit` never. `ask_person` stays pi's: on another harness it is a warning, and its runs are refused.
  - `--faux-harness MODULE` (`fauxHarness` for `startServe`) runs every harness but pi on a scripted faux harness, for tests.
- AgentRun Server's image build now proves the Codex CLI it ships is the pinned build. `docker/image-check.sh` runs `@parcha/agentrun-harness-codex`'s own check on the final image's tree: the platform package's version, the binary's sha256 and the version the binary reports must all be Codex 0.164.0's for the image's platform. A missing binary, a different binary or another release fails the build with the typed error (`CodexNotInstalled` or `CodexPinMismatch` and the pin it names). The pins already cover both platforms the image is built for, linux/amd64 and linux/arm64.
- `docker/image-smoke.sh` now checks codex. A built image must list it in `GET /v2/capabilities`. Because the image carries the pinned Codex CLI, codex is never unavailable as not installed or not the pinned build. It is available, or unavailable for a setting its reason names, such as `OPENAI_API_KEY`. Set `IMAGE_SMOKE_CODEX=available` on a run that is given a model key, and codex must then be available.
  - A new test covers what the server offers of codex. It is available on the pinned build with a model key, unavailable for the key it names without one, and unavailable as not the pinned build, with the install line, when the binary is any other build.
- **AgentRun Server's image has git, on Debian trixie**
  - `packages/serve/docker/Dockerfile` builds both stages on `node:22.23.2-trixie-slim`, pinned by its multi-arch index digest as before, and the runtime installs `git` (2.47) next to `tini`. `pi-git-remote` needs git 2.40 or newer; bookworm's is 2.39, and the bookworm image had no git at all. The build fails if the git it installs is older than 2.40.
  - The base moves from Debian 12 (bookworm, glibc 2.36) to Debian 13 (trixie, glibc 2.41). Node's version is still 22.23.2, but its binary is the trixie build. An image built `FROM` this one gets trixie's packages and glibc: whatever it adds with apt or compiles must suit trixie. A native addon built against glibc 2.41 does not load on bookworm.
  - git and its Debian dependencies (perl among them) add about 36 MB to the compressed image (181 MB to 217 MB) and about 100 MB unpacked.
- An import cuts the keys [secretlint](https://github.com/secretlint/secretlint)'s recommended rules find in what it reads (Anthropic, OpenAI, GitHub, Slack and Stripe keys, AWS secret keys beside their name, private keys, database URLs with a password, and more), as well as the environment's values and the saved keys. It reads each session file as it is and as its records' strings, so a key in a Codex shell output kept as JSON is found too. The cut reaches everything the import writes: transcripts, run titles and inputs, the agent's name and description, its instructions, its skills' text and their file paths. A key in a folder's or file's name, one split across folders among them, is kept as `redacted-<hash>`, and a skill's files stay one skill.
  - `agentrun-serve import` prints `N secrets removed` after its import line (`N secrets would be removed` with `--dry-run`), counting each distinct key once.
  - Known key formats are removed. Other secrets in your session history, like passwords in config files, are imported as they are.
  - Runs imported before this change are not cut again: a re-import skips sessions it already holds.
  - New dependencies: `@secretlint/core` and `@secretlint/secretlint-rule-preset-recommend`, both 13.1.0 (MIT).
- `agentrun-serve import` counts what it leaves out by why, on the dry run and the import alike ("left out: 84 sessions from folders that no longer exist (--include-deleted imports them too)", "left out: 2 sessions still being written: they come in next time"), in place of one `skipped <path>: <why>` line per file, which `--verbose` still prints. `--include-deleted` imports the sessions of project folders that no longer exist, such as a deleted git worktree's, each folder its own agent with no instructions or skills to read; `--all` keeps those, temporary folders' and sessions with no message of the person's, as before. Each left-out entry of an import's report carries its `kind`, and `leftOutOf` groups them.
- `import` brings in every local session with its analytics. Each imported run keeps what its session used, a run its source did not price gets a catalog estimate, and the import ends with a summary of every session it found (the ones it imported before included) and the address to open the console at. So does `--dry-run`, from what it would import:

  ```
  Found 1,284 sessions  Claude Code 911 · Codex 301 · pi 72
  Imported into 37 agents (one per project)
  + 41.2M tokens (78% cached) · 2,310 h of agent time
  + Busiest hour: 02:00 · top tool: Bash (31%)
  ```

  - What each run keeps: the store's metrics, which are its tokens, model requests, models, tool calls with their errors, the effort or thinking level it ran at, and its time at work (the gaps between the session's records, each capped at 15 minutes). The run's transcript carries each request's own tokens and model, so its timeline and its metrics agree.
  - Claude Code's usage is read once per message, though the CLI repeats it on every record of a message. Its own `<synthetic>` turns are an API error, imported as a request that failed, or a line of its own, which is no request and never the person's.
  - Codex's usage comes from its `token_usage_record`s, or from its `token_count` events in a rollout without them, with OpenAI's cached tokens taken out of the input. A Codex call fails when its output or its completed item says so.
  - pi's usage is read as pi wrote it.
  - A request whose source gave no counts stays unmetered, never 0 tokens.
  - The estimate covers every Codex session, a Claude Code session using a model the CLI could not price, and a pi session with an unpriced request. It is each request at its own provider's catalog list price for its model, in the metrics' `costUsdEst` with `costBasis: "catalog"`, and the run's cost stays unknown. A provider the catalog does not list for that model, or a request whose tokens are unknown, leaves the estimate unknown, never $0. A provider's $0 is $0. A cost the source recorded is kept, with no estimate.
  - The summary: the cached share is cache reads over all input, agent time is the runs' time at work, the busiest hour is the local hour of day with the most model requests, and the top tool is the one with the most calls. A figure with no data behind it reads `unknown`. The address to open the console at is the server's public one when `AGENTRUN_PUBLIC_URL` is set, else this machine's at the port the server listens on, which `import` now takes as `--port`.
- AgentRun Server runs Jev on the model gateway's key when that gateway is OpenRouter. With `AGENT_MODEL_BASE_URL` on the host `openrouter.ai` (exactly that host) and `AGENT_MODEL_API_KEY` set, a workflow's typed questions go to Jev through OpenRouter on `AGENT_MODEL_API_KEY`, so a server on an OpenRouter gateway needs no second key for decisions. `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` still comes first, then `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`; a gateway on any other host does not run Jev.
  - On the OpenRouter route, jev's `note` in `GET /capabilities` names the variable Jev runs on: `Jev runs on OPENROUTER_API_KEY, ...` or `Jev runs on AGENT_MODEL_API_KEY, ...`. Both are the model gateway's key, which a run's processes already hold; set `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` to keep the decision key out of them.
  - On either OpenRouter variable, the server hands the key to a workflow run's process on the decision descriptor, as it hands `TYPESAFE_API_KEY`. The run's judge reads its key there, never from its environment.
  - With no key, jev's `reason` (and the `not_set_up_here` warning and `409` message) also names the gateway route.
- A run that learns gets the skills its agent wrote beside its revision's own. Each is a folder `skills/<name>/` on the learning folder's `main`, copied into the run's `work/.agents/skills/<name>/`: pi and codex read its `SKILL.md` in their prompt, and claude-agent loads it through its Skill tool when its adapter takes it.
  - A skill named like one of the revision's is the revision's. Only an active skill is served: one whose `SKILL.md` sets `status: pending` (held for its owner's OK), or any status but `active`, is not.
  - Learned skills get their own budget, 256 KiB of files in all, and never more than the revision's skills leave of the 1 MiB an adapter takes of a run's skills, so they never push the revision's back into the prompt. The one changed last comes first, each whole while it fits; one that does not fit, or that holds one of the server's secrets, is left out.
  - The run's pin (`runs/<id>/learning.json`) names the files laid (`skills`) and the skills left out (`skills_left_out`). A follow-up gets them laid again, fresh. A learned skill the run's work no longer holds whole when its process starts is left out of what the harness gets, and the run goes on.
  - A symbolic link or a submodule at `skills/` or under it is a learning fault: the run starts with nothing learned (`seed_failed`).
  - `@parcha/agentrun-kernel` exports `SKILL_BYTES`, the 1 MiB its `skillProblems` takes of a host's skills in all.
  - A person retires a learned skill or a workflow draft as they retire a note: `POST /v2/agents/{id}/learning/files/{file}/retire` with If-Match on its `skills/<name>/SKILL.md` or its draft's `workflows/<name>/README.md` (percent-encoded). It sets `status: retired` in one host commit (`learning: retired by a person: <file>`), the learning view lists it `retired`, and no later run is given it. A stale ETag is 412, and the token may retire as it may retire a note; only an approval takes the owner's session.
- The owner approves a note that waits for their OK: `POST /agents/{agent_id}/learning/files/{file}/approve`, under If-Match, makes a pending note active, in MEMORY.md and read by every later run, and marks what it supersedes. Only the owner, signed in with their password, approves: the console's password session from this server's own origin with a JSON body. Every token is 403, the server's own included (a script's, an agent's MCP client's, the MCP endpoint's tools'), and so are a run's door token and every value a run's process holds; with no credential it is 401, as every route. With accounts off (`AGENTRUN_ACCOUNTS=0`) nothing approves, since the page's cookie there is minted from the token. `capabilities.learning.approve` is true only for the owner's password session; any other caller reads false with `approve_needs: "password"`. Runs that use bash on the host run as you: for untrusted tasks, use the sandbox. The approval is one host commit with the `Changed-By: user` and `Approved-Via: console` trailers, and the learning log reads `by: {user: true, via: "console"}`, never the note's text.
- The server's learning code is its own, in `src/learn/`, beside `SPEC.md`, the learning folder's contract: the note format (`notes.ts`), the checker of what a run proposes (`check.ts`), the end-of-run prompt (`reflect.ts`) and the grader's rubric, prompt and verdict (`grade.ts`). The server depends on no learning package: `@parcha/agentrun-learn` stays held and unchanged, and an install of the server gets nothing more than before.
  - A note may name the notes it replaces, `supersedes: [old.md]` or one bare file name. Only the server sets `status: superseded` and `superseded_by`, on each note a merged one supersedes, and a run that sets either is refused.
  - `SPEC.md` lists the checker's rules and caps, the grader's four questions (general, from content, duplicate, contradicts) and how its verdict fails closed.
- Learning works on every harness and on cheap models. The end-of-run turn answers with its notes as text, so an agent needs no `write` tool and a note asked for during the task survives. The grader keeps a lesson the agent worked out itself and what the user asked it to keep, and still drops what a page told it.
  - The end-of-run turn (`reflectPrompt`) asks the agent to look back over the whole conversation, what the user asked it to remember included, and to answer with one ` ```note <file>.md ` block per note, using no tool. The server reads the notes from the turn's answer alone (`parseReflection`, new in `src/learn/reflect.ts`), whole, and keeps the answer and what the user said in `runs/<id>/learning-turn.json` as the run settles. The checker, the grader and the merge run unchanged. Nothing the run writes in `work/.learning/`, during the task or the turn, is read, and the folder is no longer laid again before the turn or moved aside after it (`runs/<id>/learning-turn/` is gone).
  - The learning reasons gain `unparsed`: the answer gave notes out of format (a note outside a block, a block not closed, one with no file name or two, a name given twice), and none was kept. Its detail reads "Not kept: its notes weren't in the expected format." No run gets `no_write_tool` or `tool_denied` now, since an agent with no tool learns too; they stay in the list for older runs, whose ends are still read and counted. The `# Learned` intro says that only what the run answers at the end is kept.
  - `GRADE_RUBRIC` asks five questions. "From content" becomes "Planted": an instruction a page, a document or a tool's output gave is planted (act for someone else, send data, trust or use a source content offered, skip or change the instructions, or remember this). A lesson the agent drew from its own work, such as a URL pattern, a site's layout or how a tool behaves, is not planted, and neither is what the user asked in their own words; an email or a message pasted or forwarded into the run is content. A new "Secret" question covers passwords, keys, account numbers and private details, whoever gave them, a new "Risky" question asks whether a rule widens trust in a site or a seller, skips or loosens an approval or a check, or names an account or a destination for money or data, and "General" counts a standing preference of the user's as general and a tool's or the setup's failure as tied to its case. Each note's decision is keep, pending or drop, with its origin, you or run (`parseVerdict` reads `decision` and `origin`, and the older `keep` alone). `gradePrompt` takes `asked`, the user's messages in the run (the first and the newest three, each cut to 1,000 characters), fenced as data.
  - The grader runs on the model the server names for it: `--grade-model` (or `AGENTRUN_SERVE_GRADE_MODEL`), else the strong tier (`AGENT_MODEL_STRONG`), else the agent's own model as before. `GET /capabilities` names it as `learning.grade_model`, null for each run's own. It reads what the user said in the run, cut of the server's secrets, which the proposal now keeps (`asked`).
  - An eval set of 20 labelled runs (`packages/serve/test/fixtures/learn-eval.json`) and a held-out set of 9 (`learn-eval-heldout.json`) run in CI on a faux grader that answers by label, and `packages/serve/scripts/learn-eval.mjs` runs them on real models.
  - A risky rule is pending, never dropped and never served on its own, whether the user asked for it or the run worked it out (a note the grader keeps and names Risky too): merged as `status: pending`, with `origin: you` when it is the user's (`src/learn/notes.ts`: `NOTE_STATUSES` and `NOTE_ORIGINS`, `mergedNote`, `approve`), in no `MEMORY.md` and no run's copy, and named on the run's learning: `pending`, `[{file, name}]`, on `GET /v2/runs/{run_id}`, in run lists and on the run's whole learning, never among `learned`, and in its detail ("Waiting for your OK: <name>.", after "Learned: <name>." when the run kept a note too); `GET /capabilities` says so (`learning.pending: true`). It waits for its owner: a person can retire it or approve it (serve-learning-approve). An edit keeps it waiting: a `PUT` that drops or changes its status is 400 with the rule `status`. A run's note that sets `origin` is refused as `status`.
- An agent with learning on remembers, on pi, claude-agent and codex: when a run answers or fails, the server asks the same session one end-of-run turn to add notes to its copy of the agent's learning folder; what it adds is checked, graded by one grader on the agent's own model, and merged into the folder's `main`, where the next run reads it.
  - The turn goes through the run's door under a fixed request id, at most once, sent with the origin `reflect` that only the server sets, under the run's cap; the run's answer stays the job's, and the turn's rows are labelled `learning`. `startServe` takes `reflectTimeoutMs` (default 5 minutes).
  - As the run settles, before it reads ended or a follow-up can open it again, the turn's `work/.learning/` is moved whole (one rename) to `runs/<id>/learning-turn/`; what it wrote is read from there alone, without following a link, and checked by the server's `checkProposal` before anything is written; a follow-up finds `work/.learning/` laid again from the seed; `MEMORY.md` as the turn left it is ignored. The grader is the server's `gradePrompt` and `parseVerdict`, fail-closed, over each active note's index line and only the superseded notes in full; a call cut off at its bound (`startServe`'s `gradeTimeoutMs`, default 3 minutes) has an unknown cost, never $0; an injection screen runs first only with Jev's key. The merge is one host commit: new notes, superseded ones marked, `MEMORY.md` rebuilt, a `Run-Id` trailer, compare-and-swap on `main`, never twice.
  - The run's `learning` gains the states `reflecting`, `proposed` and `merged` and the drop reasons of the console's closed list; a learning timeline row (kind `learning`) is shown for every outcome, `seed_failed` included. Each run's learning end is one line of the learning ledger, `DATA/learning-usage.jsonl` (`{run, agent, state, reason, at, cost, tokens}`), written once its `serve.json` holds it; `GET /usage` reads the grades' cost from it under the meter `learning`, and `GET /stats` gains `learning: {runs, merged, dropped, in_progress}` over its filters, from the ledger and the runs the server holds, never a run's files.
  - `@parcha/pi-durable-agent`: `TimelineKind` gains `learning`.
- A run that learns reads its notes whole in its instructions under `# Learned`, so an agent with no `read` tool follows what it learned. Before, `# Learned` held only the index's one line per note, and the notes were files in `.learning/` that such an agent could never open.
  - Each active note is there whole (`## name (file.md)`, its description, then its text), the one changed last on `main` first, while they all fit the learning budget: `--learning-budget BYTES` (or `AGENTRUN_SERVE_LEARNING_BUDGET`), 8000 bytes by default. Past it, the run reads the index, then the notes changed last, whole, while they fit; an agent with the `read` tool is told the rest are in `.learning/`, and one without is not. `0` gives the index alone.
  - Only active notes are served. A note with any other status is never in the instructions, whole or by an index line a hand edit left in `MEMORY.md`, nor in the copy in `.learning/`, and it is not counted toward the budget.
  - Every name, description, text and index line the run reads is cut of the server's secrets as they are when the run is made, in its instructions and in its copy in `.learning/`.
  - A run's pin (`runs/<id>/learning.json`) keeps what it reads as `section`. A pin an earlier server wrote, the index alone as `learned`, is read as that server gave it.
- A person sees and controls what an agent has learned, through the API (`learning/1`): `GET /v2/agents/{agent_id}/learning` (the index, the notes, the log of changes by run or by person, and the runs' counts), its `notes` and `log` pages, one note as text with its `ETag`, and `GET /v2/runs/{run_id}/learning` (with the grader's verdict and its item for each new note: kept or not, the rubric's question it failed, and why) and `.../learning/diff` for one run.
  - `PUT /v2/agents/{agent_id}/learning/files/{file}` takes the note's whole text (as text, or as JSON `{text}` from the console's page) and edits a note (`If-Match`) or creates one (`If-None-Match: *`), and `POST .../retire` retires one; a stale ETag is 412, a write with no precondition 428 (`precondition_required`, a new error code). A person's note passes the same structural checks as a run's, with no grader, and lands as one host commit on `main` with a `Changed-By: user` trailer and `MEMORY.md` rebuilt. A refused note is 400 with `problems` on the error; a retire checks only the note's format (its front matter, and the cap with the status line on top), never its secrets, so a leaking note can always be revoked. The view and the pages read a commit's notes once (kept for the few commits last read), and a log page reads only its own commits. A file name holding one of the server's secrets (a value made secret after the note was written) reads `[redacted]` in the notes, the log and a run's diff.
  - The server's learning code (`src/learn/`) gains `checkNote(path, bytes, refuseValues)`, which checks one note by itself, as a person writes it, and `retire(text)`, which marks a note retired.
- An agent whose definition says `learning: true` starts every run from what it has learned, on pi, claude-agent and codex alike. Learning is off unless the agent says so, and the server's `--learning off` (or `AGENTRUN_SERVE_LEARNING=off`) turns it off for every run; capabilities report the switch as `features.learning`.
  - The agent's learning folder, `learning/` in its home (`DATA/homes/<key>/learning/`, the key as the home's), is in Claude Code's auto-memory format: `MEMORY.md`, one line per active note (`- [name](file.md) — description`), and one `<slug>.md` per note with `name`, `description`, `type` (`user`, `feedback`, `project`, `reference`) and optionally `status: superseded | retired` with `superseded_by`. It is plain git with `main` checked out, made by the agent's first run that learns with an empty `MEMORY.md`, and the server is its only writer, with hooks, fsmonitor, lazy fetches and system and global config off.
  - Each run pins `main`'s commit in `runs/<id>/learning.json`, kept through a resume or a follow-up. The first 200 lines of that commit's `MEMORY.md` go into the run's instructions under `# Learned`, and `MEMORY.md` and the active notes are copied into the run's `work/.learning/`.
  - A learning fault never blocks a run: a folder that cannot be made or read starts the run with nothing learned. The run's `learning` reads `{state: "seeded"}`, `{state: "dropped", reason: "seed_failed"}`, or `null` for a run that does not learn; run lists carry `{state, reason}`. A server that goes down while it copies the notes into a run has saved the run's input first, so the next one relaunches the run with its first message and nothing learned; a copy it finds there unsaved goes, and the run's learning reads `seed_failed`.
  - `@parcha/pi-durable-agent`: an agentic revision keeps `learning` when its input sets it, so a definition that leaves it out keeps its revision id.
- Learning you can see, through the API (`learning/2`, additive): a run's `learning` block, on `GET /v2/runs/{run_id}` and in every run list, gains `used` (the notes the run's job read from `.learning/`, found once as it settles from its timeline on any harness and kept in `serve.json`, never `MEMORY.md` nor a read of its end-of-run turn) and `learned` (the notes its merge added, with the notes each superseded), each absent when it holds no note and cut of the server's secrets as they are now. `GET /v2/agents/{agent_id}/learning` gains `impact`: the agent's completed runs before its first note against those that started from one, each side's run count, median time to the answer (before the run looks back), median job cost without the look-back (with `cost_runs`, the runs whose cost is known) and median of what learning itself cost a run (`median_learning_usd`: its look-back and its grade), from the runs' rows and the learning ledger (whose line now carries `seed_notes`, `answered_at`, `job_cost_usd` and `look_back_usd`), null unless both sides count 2 runs. Each note in the agent's view and its pages carries its `etag`, the one its file's GET answers, and each run list's row carries the run's learning `detail`, cut to 140 characters. Every learning `detail`, and the run's learning row in its timeline, which now says the same, is in a person's words ("Started from 2 notes.", "Learned: Check units (replaces 1 older note)", "Nothing new to remember from this run.", "Not kept: <the grader's reason, its first sentence>"); the reason codes do not change. The end-of-run turn's message reads "Looking back on this run." in the timeline, never the prompt the model is sent, and `GET /v2/runs/{run_id}/learning` carries `used` and `learned` too.
- The MCP tools `agents_create` and `agents_revise` take an optional `definition`: any other definition field, passed through as it is to `POST /v2/agents` or the definition `PUT`, whose own checks answer for each. A field the server takes later (an agent's `place` now) needs no change to the tools, and a field the route refuses is its own `422`, by field. A field named outright wins over the same one in `definition`, and `agents_revise` keeps every field neither names.
- A coding agent can set up AgentRun Server and drive it. `POST /v2/mcp` is an MCP endpoint, Streamable HTTP in its simplest form: one JSON-RPC message per POST, answered as JSON, with no stream and no session. It has ten tools: `whoami`, `agents_list`, `agents_create`, `agents_revise`, `runs_start`, `runs_get` (with a waiting run's question and its console link), `runs_events`, `runs_files`, `runs_respond` and `runs_cancel`. Each calls the `/v2` routes in process with the caller's token, so it checks and answers as they do: the route's JSON is the result, and a refusal is a tool error holding the route's `error`. A run is started, then polled; no call is held open for one. The endpoint takes the Bearer token only, never the page's session cookie, refuses a browser `Origin` other than the server's own (`403`), and refuses any method but POST (`405`). `GET /v2/runs/{id}/timeline` takes `limit` (1 to 500 rows a page). `GET /llms.txt`, public, gives the server in brief, and the package ships `skills/agentrun-serve/SKILL.md` for a coding agent: check the server, the token, connect with `claude mcp add` or `codex mcp add`, create an agent, run it and answer it. The served OpenAPI document lists the endpoint as `callMcp` (`mcp/1`) and the timeline's `limit` as `timelinelimit/1`.
- Remote MCP servers can be previewed and saved through `/v2/settings/mcp-servers`. `POST .../preview` connects over Streamable HTTP with the official MCP SDK client (2026-07-28 first, 2025-era servers by their `initialize`), lists every tool and stores nothing; it answers `422 auth_required` with `details.auth` `bearer` or `oauth`, `422 not_mcp`, or `424 upstream_failed`, and never follows a redirect. A server is kept with at most 500 tools and 1 MiB of them; past either it is `422` and nothing is kept. `PUT .../{name}` previews, then keeps the URL, the tool snapshot and the credential (`none`, `bearer` or named `headers`), each value sealed under `AGENTRUN_SEAL_KEY`; with no seal key a server that takes a credential is `409 no_sealing_key`. Reads say `credential: sealed | seal_key_missing | unreadable` and never show a value; a `PUT` without the credential keeps the sealed one while the auth type and the URL's origin are unchanged. `POST .../{name}/refresh` answers what was added, removed and changed; `DELETE` forgets the server. A saved-servers file that does not read is never written over: reads say `file_unreadable`, and writes are `409 store_unreadable`. Capabilities gain `features.mcp_client`. OAuth sign-in (`auth.type: "oauth"`) is refused with reason `not_yet`. Agents do not use these servers' tools yet.
- A model provider's key is saved from the page like a tool key: `PUT /v2/settings/tool-keys/{anthropic|openai|openrouter}`, rows of `kind: "model"` in `GET /v2/settings/tool-keys` (the tool keys are `kind: "tool"`), kept sealed the same way. Every run gets it, as every run gets the launcher's model keys. With no `AGENT_MODEL`, such a key, saved or the server's own, now gives the default model, the first of `anthropic/claude-sonnet-5-5`, `openai/gpt-6.1-sol` and `openrouter/anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5` whose key is here: each its provider's capable everyday model, not its dearest. claude-agent takes only the Anthropic one, codex only the OpenAI one, each harness's `default_model` in capabilities shows the one it gets, and `AGENT_MODEL` still wins for a harness that runs it: one that can't (claude-agent on a gateway's own id) takes its own key's default. Capabilities' `models` gain `ready`, whether a revision that names no model runs here, and `needs` (`key` or `model`, null when ready); both change with a key's save or delete, with no restart. A run or a save that finds no model says what a person does, in place of operator flags: "No model yet: add a model key in Settings → Model keys, or set ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, OPENAI_API_KEY or OPENROUTER_API_KEY where AgentRun starts." (and for a named model whose provider has no key, that provider's key).
- A monid key saved through the settings API puts monid's catalog in an agent's hands on every harness, with no restart, only in the runs that name a monid tool, and monid's charges in the run's cost and under its spend cap.

  - A tool key saved through `PUT /v2/settings/tool-keys/{id}` is read by name like a secret the launcher hands over, the launcher's value winning for a name it holds. Each run gets the saved keys at its launch, over the same pipe as the launcher's secrets (a workflow run's `PARALLEL_API_KEY` on its decision descriptor), and never in any process's environment; no run gets the seal key. A value that does not open is treated as not set: it never crashes the server, and nothing unsealed stands in for it. A `PUT` or `DELETE` needs no restart: capabilities, the harnesses' offers, a save's checks and a run's checks see the change at once. A run already running keeps what it was handed, and the next launch takes what is saved then. Capabilities give each tool's key as `tools[].key`, a `GET /settings/tool-keys` id (`browserbase` for `browser`). `BROWSERBASE_PROJECT_ID` joins the launcher's list of secrets, as every field a tool key holds does. `agentrun-serve import` reads the launcher's secrets by name and opens the saved keys in its data directory, and cuts both out of what it writes. Before this, the launcher's secrets were not cut, because the import read them from an environment they had already been moved out of.
  - A run's process gets a tool key's fields (`MONID_API_KEY`, `BROWSERBASE_API_KEY` and `BROWSERBASE_PROJECT_ID`) over its secrets pipe only when its revision names a tool the key unlocks, as `GET /v2/settings/tool-keys` lists them: an agentic revision's `tools`, or the tools a workflow's model and call nodes name. Until now every run's process held every tool key the server held, whatever its tools. The tools are read from the revision the run is pinned to, at every launch: a relaunch, and a run opened again after a server restart, get the same keys as its first launch. The model keys still reach every run, and the decision keys go as before. A `typesafe` key (`TYPESAFE_API_KEY`) is saved the same way and unlocks Jev's decisions (`decisions.jev`). Each key row has a kind: only a `tool` row's fields are scoped this way, and a key of any other kind reaches every run.
  - `monid.discover`, `monid.inspect` and `monid.run` run on claude-agent and codex too, as host tools in the run's process: the same calls and answers as on pi, on the key the run's process gets over its pipe, a long answer filed under the run's `work/evidence/monid/`. The harness calls and journals each under its wire name (`monid_discover`, `monid_inspect`, `monid_run`), as pi does, and the run's timeline shows it so. A `monid.run` call a crash cut off is answered interrupted, never sent again; `discover` and `inspect` may run again. Capabilities and the monid key's row list every harness for the three, and a claude-agent or codex revision naming one saves with no warning that it runs on pi only.
  - What a `monid.run` charged is the run's spend, on every harness. It is booked once in the run's own records as a tool cost (`agentrun.tool_cost`, `TOOL_COST` from `@parcha/pi-durable-agent/timeline`: `{tool: "monid.run", charged_by: "monid", usd}`), which the timeline adds to the run's cost and shows on a `phase` row of its own (`Tool cost: monid charged $0.0030 for monid.run`); the usage row counts it apart from the model requests. A run that may have charged and reports no cost books an unknown charge, never $0: the run's cost reads `null`, and under a cap every later request is refused. Each run is marked before it is sent (`agentrun.tool_started`, `TOOL_STARTED`), held at the price an earlier `monid.inspect` in the run quoted, else at a $1 allowance; its charge settles the mark, and a mark a crash left unsettled leaves the run's cost unknown, as a cut model request does, while the cap counts it at its held figure, never $0. `GET /v2/usage` grouped by meter shows the charges as their own meter, `tools` (capabilities' `usage.meters`). Under a `max_cost_usd`, each `monid.run` is held as a model request is: unless the run's spend plus what the run is held at (the quote, else the $1 allowance) fits the cap, or when the spend is unknown, the call is refused with a `spend_cap_reached` error the model reads, naming its price and the cap (and that an inspect gives a quote when the allowance was used), and monid is not called. Runs go one at a time under a cap; on claude-agent and codex the check also holds each model request still in flight at its worst case. `discover` and `inspect` cost nothing and are never refused.
- `monid.discover`, `monid.inspect` and `monid.run`: with `MONID_API_KEY` set, an agent on pi gets monid's whole catalog of paid data endpoints through three tools in the tool picker. It finds an endpoint in plain words, reads its input schemas and price, and runs it, paid per call from the monid wallet. A run is sent once and never retried; an async one is read until it ends (about two minutes at most) and stopped at monid when the run stops. An answer over 60,000 characters is cut, the whole of it filed under the run's `work/evidence/monid/`. The launcher hands `MONID_API_KEY` to the server and each run's process over their pipes, like the other secrets. pi only for now: a revision naming one on claude-agent or codex saves with a warning and its runs are refused.
- A profile pack (version 1) and its resolver, not yet wired to any route. A pack maps each effort (`low`, `medium`, `high`) to an ordered list of skill names and an optional thinking level, and each output type to its writer skills, the workspace file the output lands in, and optional per-effort writer skills. `packIssues` returns every issue with a pack as `422` field issues pointing into it (`effort.low.skills[0]`), refusing unknown keys, skill names that are not 1 to 64 lowercase letters, digits and dashes starting with a letter or digit (the Agent Skills name rule, so nested, spaced, dot-leading and uppercase names are refused), output files that are not plain relative workspace paths, and lists or output maps over 32 entries. `resolvePack` reads a checked pack for one run: effort defaults to `medium`, the output to the pack's `default_output`, and the skills are the effort row's then the writer's, each once in first-seen order.
- AgentRun Server is published to npm, and `@parcha/agentrun-decisions` with it, which the server uses: `npx @parcha/agentrun-serve` starts the server with nothing else installed, its console included. pi runs; the Claude Agent SDK and Codex stay optional peers a person installs beside it, and capabilities say for each that it is not installed and the line that installs it. The archive carries the console with `dist/console/THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt`, which the console's build writes from the bundle's own module list (each bundled package, its version, license and license text), and the server's `NOTICE` points to it. The release builds the console and stops when the server's archive lacks the console or its notices.
- An agentic run is told of the files given to it. Its first message, on every harness, is the input's `content`, then a blank line and one line naming each file in its `work/inputs/` by the path its tools take, with its size: `Files for this run: inputs/notes.txt (32 B), inputs/docs/plan.md (1.5 KB).`, at most 20 and then how many more, and never past what the run's door takes in one message. A run asked to "summarize the attached file" used to be given the file but never its name. The run's `input` is stored as it was sent; the timeline's first `user_message` is the message the harness got, the line included. A run given no files is sent its `content` as before.
- **A workflow run on AgentRun Server is drawn live, pauses and resumes, and a workflow is checked before it is saved**
  - `GET /v2/runs/{id}/graph`: the run's workflow and graph, each node's state (map items labelled from the run's input, with the branch each route took for them; a route's branch), the decisions with their receipts (state seen, images, evidence, cost basis, error), the calls with their input and output, and totals. While the run goes it reads a live view the run's process writes (`live.json`), so a poll never waits on the run's store or answers `home_busy`.
  - `POST /v2/runs/{id}/pause` and `/resume`: what is in flight finishes, and nothing new starts until the resume; a paused run stays paused across a restart. Run status gains `paused` (marked `pause/1`), `GET /runs?status=paused` lists them, and `GET /stats` counts a paused run as active.
  - `POST /v2/workflows/validate { workflow, tools? }` answers `{ ok, issues: [{ path, code, message }], warnings }` and writes nothing. A refused save's `422` lists every issue as a field (`definition.workflow/<path>`) with its `code`.
  - A workflow run's title is its agent's name and a few words of its input, never its JSON. A run's `llm` calls count in its cost.
  - pi-durable-agent: `workflowIssues(workflow, tools?)` lists every issue a save would refuse; `checkWorkflowBody`'s refusal carries them, each with its message.
  - dsl: the recovery driver's `pinJson()` gives its frame as it stands, for a host's live view.
- A run's end writes its metrics to the agent store, each time it is carried back: its tokens, model requests, models and tool calls over every conversation of its store (a workflow's model nodes and a harness's session included), the effort its newest process ran at (the run's `effort`, else its revision's `thinking`, else the `AGENT_THINKING` that process started with, else medium; codex's max is xhigh), and on claude-agent or codex the model its revision names, since their journal names the harness. A follow-up's end replaces them, and a run whose process died gets them from what its store holds. Metrics the store refuses (a tool name past its limit) leave the run ended without them, never unended. A workflow's decisions are not in its token counts yet.
- A run, and its row in `GET /v2/runs` and `GET /v2/agents/{id}/runs`, carries `pending`: what it waits on, as one field told apart by `kind`, or null. A run waiting on a person carries `{kind: "question", question, asked_at}`. It is read from the run's own last row, the one its status reads, never from a scan of its timeline, so a list shows what each run waits on without a request per run, and the MCP tool `runs_get` returns it as the route does: a run with any number of timeline rows reports its current question. The served OpenAPI document gives `pending` as a union on `kind` (`pending/1`), so a later kind, a tool call waiting on approval, is one more variant of it.
- A run's timeline says when its process resumed it: "Resumed after a crash" when its last process died holding it and the server relaunched it, or "Resumed after the server stopped" when it drained at a stop and the next start opened it again. The row comes from an entry the new process writes on the run's root as it opens (`RUN_RESUMED`, exported by `@parcha/pi-durable-agent/timeline`), so it stays once the run ends, on pi, claude-agent and codex alike. A first open, a follow-up and a wake from a park write none. A reader from before this ignores the entry.
- An escalated workflow run reads as waiting for a person, not as failed. Its `status` is `completed` (state `idle`), with `error` and `result.output` null, and a new nullable `review` on the run and on the runs list: `{kind, node, reason, at}`. `kind` is the escalation's kind as its escalate node names it, `node` is the label of the node it escalated at, `reason` is the escalation's summary, and `at` is when it ended. Every other run's `review` is null. The field is this server's own on the `/v2` document (`Run.review`, `RunSummary.review`, marked `x-agentrun-serve: review/1`). A run ended before this kept no review: it reads its row's summary as the reason, with kind and node null. A workflow run still takes no messages (501 `unsupported_here`). `readWorkflowRun` gains `escalation: {kind, label, summary}`.
- `web_search` and `web_fetch` work on pi, `claude-agent` and `codex` with only a model key. Each resolves in one order: the provider the user picked (the agent's own `definition.tool_settings` over the server's `PUT /v2/settings/tools`), then the native tool, then the kernel's keyless fallback. `web_search` is `auto` by default, native where there is one: Claude's own WebSearch (its adapter is opened with it, and no `web_search` host tool is offered), and on pi one request to the run's own model provider with its hosted search (Anthropic's `web_search_20250305` at 5 searches, OpenAI's Responses `web_search`), read from the provider's events and booked as one of the run's requests, its tokens plus $0.01 a search. On codex it is Codex's own search, each search booked at $0.01, except behind a gateway or under a spend cap, where it is keyless Exa; on claude-agent under a spend cap it is keyless Exa too, since Claude's own search has per-use fees a cap cannot reserve yet; a pi model behind a gateway or on another provider likewise searches Exa's public MCP endpoint with no key (`exa-keyless`, at most 8 results; a 429 at its free limit, or a 403 for a connection that is not direct IPv4, answers `{error}`, never retried). Every provider answers one shape, `{provider, query, results: [{url, title, snippet, published?}], text?}`, snippets cut at 3,000 characters, and the model's copy ends with a line asking it to cite the URLs it relies on; Parallel's search answers it too. `web_fetch` is the kernel's guarded fetch by default, and a page whose text it cuts at 60,000 characters says `truncated: true` with `original_chars` ; Parallel (search and extract) runs only where it is picked, and only then is its key handed to the run; a picked Parallel that gives no page leaves the direct fetch's page saying why (`parallel`), never a silent fallback. A pick of Parallel without `PARALLEL_API_KEY` resolves `unavailable`, and a run naming the tool is refused (`409`). Settings are `{web_search?: {provider: auto|native|exa-keyless|parallel|off}, web_fetch?: {provider: kernel|parallel}}` (`off` turns `web_search` off: nothing is searched, and a run naming it is refused, `409`), kept in the data directory (`tool-settings.json`, 0600); a `permission` is refused (`422 not_yet`) until the tool gate, and `capabilities.features.tool_permissions` is false. `GET /v2/capabilities` gives the two tools a `resolves` per harness, with the providers offered, the server's (`provider`) and the default (`default_provider`): capabilities is the one tool catalog (an unavailable tool says why, `reason`: a provider pick's own words such as `web_search is turned off in settings`, or `Needs` and the variables it is missing), and `GET /v2/agents/{id}` projects it onto the agent as `tools` (each granted tool in the same order; for one with a choice the provider in force, `provider_from`, `default_provider` and `resolved` on its harness and model). Under a run's cap pi's own search request is bounded at its context plus 125,000 input tokens and its most fee (Claude's and Codex's own searches do not run under a cap), its booked cost stops the next request, and a run whose booked spend passes its cap shows one timeline row saying by how much. In `@parcha/pi-durable-agent` a revision keeps `toolSettings`, and the timeline counts any request marked with a string `request` label as a side request, as it did a compaction's summary, and a `web_search` that answers `{error}`, or a `web_fetch` page followed by its line for the model that is `ok: false`, reads as a failed call, in its row and in the run's tool counts. With no key and no native search, web_search falls back to Exa's public keyless MCP endpoint, a third-party service with its own unpublished rate limits (a 429 past them) and terms (https://exa.ai/terms), reached over direct IPv4: set `web_search` to `native` (native only) server-wide or in an agent's `tool_settings` to turn it off, or pick `parallel` with `PARALLEL_API_KEY` to search with a key. `auto`, the default, is native where there is one, else keyless Exa. Upgrading: a server holding `PARALLEL_API_KEY` used to read every `web_fetch` page through Parallel's extract on its own; now it is the guarded direct fetch unless Parallel is picked, so set `PUT /v2/settings/tools {"web_fetch": {"provider": "parallel"}}` to keep that. A run keeps the tools its first launch resolved for its life, and a workflow's model and call nodes' tools resolve whether or not its revision lists them. `claudeAgentAdapter` takes `webSearchAllowance`: with its web search on, a search request is bounded at its body plus that many input tokens, instead of having no bound. `fetch_page` leaves the tool table: it is a legacy name for `web_fetch`, so a revision or call node naming it still saves and runs `web_fetch` under that name, resolved as `web_fetch` is.
- **`agentrun-serve import`: your Claude Code, Codex and pi sessions as agents and their runs**
  - `agentrun-serve import [--data DIR] [--from claude-code,codex,pi] [--dry-run] [--all]` reads the sessions in your own home (`~/.claude/projects`, `~/.codex/sessions`, `~/.pi/agent/sessions`) into the agent store, once, when you run it. Nothing leaves the machine.
  - Each project directory becomes one agent. Each harness seen in it becomes one revision: `claude-agent`, `codex` or `pi`, on that harness's latest model. Its instructions are the project's `CLAUDE.md` or `AGENTS.md`, and its skills are the project's skill folders (`.agents/skills`, `.claude/skills`, `.codex/skills`, `.pi/skills`), file by file. Every file is read through no link and within a size cap. A link, a binary file, an oversized file or a folder with no `SKILL.md` is left out, and the report names it.
  - Each session becomes one run, ended as it ended (`completed`, `failed` or `stopped`), at its own times, with its transcript as the run's timeline. Its cost is the source's when it records one (Claude Code's session cost, the sum of pi's request costs); otherwise it is unknown, never 0.
  - An imported run is history. A message to it is `409 not_continuable` with `details.reason` `imported`. A stop or a cancel answers as for an ended run and writes nothing. The run and its summary carry `imported_from` (`x-agentrun-serve: imported/1`).
  - The values of key-shaped environment variables (`*_API_KEY`, `*_TOKEN`, `*SECRET*`) are cut out of everything written, and each run's files are the owner's only (0600 in a 0700 directory).
  - Run again, it imports only the sessions it does not hold. A session written in the last 10 minutes waits for the next import. Subagent sessions are not imported. Projects whose directory is gone or temporary are left out unless `--all`. `--dry-run` lists each project and writes nothing.
- Setup can import this machine's Claude Code, Codex and pi sessions. It runs only on a server on that machine: listening on loopback, with no public address.
  - `GET /v2/setup/import`: before the owner exists, this server's own page reads at once that it can import. It is `202 { counting: true }` while the count runs, then `{ projects, sessions }`, or `null` for each when the count failed. Any other read is a `403`. A server the CLI starts with no owner counts as it starts, so the numbers are usually ready when the page opens. Once the owner is set, the signed-in owner reads how the import asked for at setup stands: `{ state: "running" }`, `{ state: "done", agents, runs, skipped }` or `{ state: "stopped" }`. Everyone else, and a server at a public address, gets a `404`.
  - `POST /v2/setup` takes `import: true` to import once the password is set, and then answers `importing: true`. Agents arrive as the import goes. A server at a public address, or one listening off loopback, refuses it with a `422` (field `import`) and sets no owner.
  - The owner's yes is kept in the data directory (`.import-at-setup`) until the import ends, so a server stopped mid-import goes on with it at its next start. Closing the server stops the import between two sessions and waits for it, so nothing writes the store after close.
  - A session imported before this change whose id held a saved Tool key keeps that raw id: a re-import adds a second run under `redacted-<hash>` and leaves the first. There is no migration; delete the old run to clear it.
- `GET /v2/stats` answers a dashboard's first page in one request: how many agents there are, the runs in all and how many are active, need input, failed or have a status this server cannot tell, each agent's run count, the total cost, and what is archived. It is this server's own route, outside the contract, marked `x-agentrun-serve: "stats/1"` in `/v2/openapi.json` with a closed schema. A running run is counted in exactly one of `active`, `needs_input` and `status_unknown`; one this server does not hold is `status_unknown`. The total cost sums only the runs that ended with a known cost and is null when none did, never $0. `unknown_runs` counts the rest. Archived agents and runs are left out of every number and counted on their own. Every number comes from SQL aggregates over `store.sqlite`, and no run's files are opened.
  The agent store gains `stats()`, which answers those counts and sums, and an index on `runs` (`runs_by_state`) added without a new store version. An earlier version still opens the file.
- The tools a revision may name are one closed table: each name is how the run's process binds it and the environment variables it needs, and nothing outside the table can be named or loaded. `GET /v2/capabilities` now lists `tools` as objects, `{name, summary, nodes, available}` with `missing` (the variables' names) when a tool is not available here, where it listed names; a run of a revision that names a tool whose variables are not set is refused with `409 agent_not_runnable` and `details.missing`. `read`, `write`, `edit` and `ask_person` are the table's first entries, unchanged, and the page marks a tool the server cannot bind with the variables it needs.
- Uploads: `POST /v2/files` reserves a file and `PUT /v2/files/{file_id}` takes its bytes, and a run's `file_ids` reach its workspace. Both routes answered `501` before. The bytes are streamed to disk (the 1 MiB JSON body cap does not apply), checked against the reserved `size_bytes` and `sha256` (`422 input_invalid`), and capped at `limits.max_upload_bytes`, 50 MiB (`413`). A run names up to `limits.max_files_per_run`, 20, ready files; an unknown, pending or repeated one is `422` at `file_ids` before anything starts. Each is copied into the run's `work/inputs/` at its `path`, folders kept, before the run's process starts; a name already taken gets a suffix (`notes-2.txt`). Capabilities now say `features.files.upload: true`.
  The page's session cookie may send a file's bytes with any content type to `PUT /v2/files/{file_id}`, still only with the page's own `Origin`. A route takes that rule only by a `raw` mark in the route table, which only a `PUT` may carry; every other write from the page still needs `application/json`, and the token is unchanged.
- A revision may name `web_fetch` on pi, `claude-agent` and `codex`: `fetch_page`'s Page plus `via`, `"parallel"` or `"direct"`. With `PARALLEL_API_KEY` set it reads the page through Parallel's extract (about $0.001 a URL); without the key, or when Parallel lists the URL in its errors, answers 5xx or times out, it is `fetch_page`'s guarded direct fetch. A URL the public-host guard refuses is never sent to Parallel, and a 401 or 403 stops Parallel for the rest of the run. An agentic run gets the Parallel key, on its decision descriptor and in memory only, when its revision names `web_fetch`; `fetch_page` is unchanged and never calls Parallel.
- `web_search` searches the web on pi with no key, through Exa's public MCP endpoint (`https://mcp.exa.ai/mcp`). Only the query and the number of results leave the server, over IPv4, nothing else from the run. Past Exa's free rate limit the search is a tool error naming the 429 and its Retry-After; a search ends at 60 s. Keyless Exa is the default for a harness with no native search when no search key is saved; turning it off comes with Settings → Tools, as the provider pick for each capability. The Parallel key no longer lists `web_search` in what it unlocks. claude-agent and codex do not bind `web_search` yet.
- `web_search`'s default, `auto`, uses your Parallel key when the server has one (`PARALLEL_API_KEY`, or a key saved on
  Tool keys), and bills it. That applies wherever the run has no native search on auto: pi behind a gateway or on a model
  with no hosted search, codex behind a gateway or under a spend cap, a workflow, and claude-agent. On claude-agent, `auto`
  now skips Claude's own search, which is an explicit `native` pick (refused under a spend cap, as before). Otherwise it is
  keyless Exa. The order is the pick, then native (not on claude-agent), then Parallel, then keyless Exa. A pick of
  `native` stays native only, a pick of `exa-keyless` stays keyless Exa, and `web_fetch` stays the kernel's fetch by
  default.
  Capabilities' `web_search` and `web_fetch` now say `why` beside `resolves`, one sentence per harness.
  Keyless Exa is also sturdier:
  - It is dialled over IPv4 alone, so a host whose default route is IPv6 no longer gets Exa's 403.
  - Past Exa's free limit (a 429, or the rate-limited notice Exa sends inside a 200), the search is tried once more after
    a short jittered wait, then answers "Free web search is rate-limited right now. Try again in a minute, or add a
    Parallel key in Settings → Tool keys to search with a key."
  - Exa's own failure text reaches the model as its first sentence only, quoted. Exa's instructions to update an MCP URL
    never do.
- A webhook tells you when a run needs its person and when it ends. `PUT /v2/settings/webhook` with `{url}` sets one URL; `run.needs_input` (the run is `input_required`, `data.pending.kind` `question` or `stopped`) and `run.ended` (completed, failed or cancelled) are POSTed to it as thin `{type, timestamp, data: {run_id, agent_id, status, title, url}}` messages, signed by the Standard Webhooks spec (`webhook-id`, `webhook-timestamp`, `webhook-signature: v1,<base64 HMAC-SHA256 of "{id}.{timestamp}.{body}">`) so any of its libraries verifies them. The `whsec_` secret is made at the first PUT, shown once, and kept sealed under `AGENTRUN_SEAL_KEY`, as tool keys are. A try not answered 2xx in 15 s fails, and no redirect is followed; a failed message is tried again on the spec's schedule cut at about a day, then logged as failed, and a 410 turns the webhook off. Pending messages survive a restart (`DIR/webhook-outbox.json`). `GET /v2/settings/webhook/deliveries` lists the last 50 attempts, `POST .../deliveries/{webhook_id}/redeliver` sends one again under the same `webhook-id`, and `POST .../test` sends a `webhook.test`. The routes are in the OpenAPI document as this server's own (`webhook/1`), and capabilities say `features.webhooks`.
- `GET /v2/stats` answers `analytics`: the runs' tokens, requests, time and tool calls summed from the metrics the agent store keeps, over the runs not archived that the new optional `from`, `to` (RFC 3339 date-times) and `agent` query parameters pick (400 when one is malformed). It gives `totals` with coverage (`runs` beside `runs_with_metrics`, so a run with no metrics never reads as 0 tokens), `time`, `by_harness_effort`, `by_day` (UTC), `by_source` (`live` or the source an imported run came from) and the top 20 `tools`. Every other field of the answer is unchanged and stays the whole store's. The store reads it with `store.analytics({ from?, to?, agent? })`, SQL over `store.sqlite` alone in one snapshot; a store without the metrics tables answers with none. The served document marks the addition `stats/2`.
- A key saved through `PUT /v2/settings/tool-keys/{id}` is checked against its provider before it is kept, with one call that costs nothing and a 5 s deadline: monid's discover, Parallel's list of monitors, Browserbase's list of projects, TypeSafe's list of models. A key the provider refuses (401) is `422 input_invalid` (reason `refused_by_provider`), in plain words with the provider's first sentence and never any of the key, and is not kept. Otherwise it is kept, and `GET /v2/settings/tool-keys` gives the row of each key stored here a `check`: `{state: "accepted", at}`, or `{state: "not_checked", reason, at}` when the provider would not answer the check (403: a key scoped away from that call may still work for its tools), did not answer in time, could not be reached, answered a redirect (never followed) or something else, or has no free call. A key saved before this change reads `not_checked` until it is saved again.
- A tool's key is saved by name through the settings API and kept only as ciphertext, opened only by the server on its host. `GET /v2/settings/tool-keys` lists the keys (`parallel`, `monid`, `browserbase`), what each unlocks and each field's `set` and `source`, never a value; `PUT /v2/settings/tool-keys/{id}` with `{values: {NAME: "..."}}` seals each value with AES-256-GCM, bound to its field's name, into `DIR/tool-keys.json` (0600), and `DELETE` forgets it. The seal key is `AGENTRUN_SEAL_KEY`, which the launcher takes from the environment or keeps in `agentrun/seal.key` under `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` (or `~/.config`), made the first time, outside the data directory; a run's process never gets it. With none, nothing is saved (`409 no_sealing_key`) and `capabilities.features.tool_keys.at_rest` is `unavailable`. A field the server is started with is `409 key_set_by_server`. A stored value that does not open (no seal key, another one, a damaged file) never stops the server: GET marks it `stored_unreadable` and `reenter` says why, and the log says so once, naming the file. `MONID_API_KEY` joins the launcher's secrets. pi-durable-agent exports the vault's `seal` and `unseal`, unchanged, for a host's own secrets.
  In a container, pass `AGENTRUN_SEAL_KEY` or mount a directory at `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`: the container's own `~/.config` goes with it, and a new one's fresh seal key does not open the keys the volume holds.
- Claude and Codex runs keep their token counts and their model in the run's journal, as pi runs do. A `runner.turn` record now keeps its request's tokens (`usage: {input, output, cacheRead, cacheWrite}`), so a request re-booked when the run opens again keeps them; a cost written late (`agentrun.request_cost_late`) and a compaction's cost (`agentrun.request_cost`) carry the request's tokens in `data.usage`; and an adapter answer's `pi.assistant` entry names the model its request asked for, its provider still the harness. Tokens that are not known are left out, never written as counts. Journals written before this change open and re-book as before, with no tokens. No cost changes.
- A capped `claude-agent` or `codex` run goes on after one of its requests was cut, by a stop or by a crash, while its cap leaves room: the cut request counts at the worst case the cap held for it, not as a spend no one knows. Before, the model proxy booked a response cut short at an unknown cost and a crash left a request with no booking, so the run's spend became unknown and every later request was refused with `spend_cap_reached`.
  - A harness runner's `beforeRequest` may answer `{ holdUsd }`, what the host holds for the request. The runner writes it on the request's `runner.request` record (`heldUsd`) before the request leaves, and the spend it shows the host (`spentUsd`) counts a request at that figure when the request's own cost never comes in: a response cut short, or a request a crash cut off, read by a later process. A request with no such figure (a session written before this release, or a host that holds none) leaves the spend unknown, as before. The cost booked for the request is still unknown, so the run's shown cost is unchanged.
  - serve's cap holds each request it admits at its worst case (its measured input bound and its model's output ceiling, at the model's price) and hands that figure to the runner. A capped run whose process died mid-request is opened again and goes on at that figure, and is refused with the cap's message once the figure leaves no room.
- A run whose follow-up turn ends without showing an answer now ends completed, with `result.final_answer` `""`: the turn's own answer, which has no text. The earlier answer stays in the timeline. The Claude CLI's `/compact` is one such turn: a summary request and no text. Before, the run sat `working`/`idle` until another message came, because serve ended a run only when its newest row was an answer. Now a claude-agent or codex turn writes its end on the run's root, and the run ends when every message it took has had its turn, whatever the door said meanwhile. A follow-up placed while a turn works is still waited for, on every harness. A claude-agent or codex run whose process died after a turn's last writes, before the turn's end was written, ends once its next process opens: that process writes the missing end when the session holds the turn as over, completed when the turn's answer is on the run's root and interrupted when it is not, so a turn that showed no answer leaves the run waiting for the person (`input_required`). When a message the person sent meanwhile is still waiting, that process starts the message's turn first and writes the missing end inside it, so the run completes only once that message is answered, also when the server restarted with it. A message accepted and not sent yet keeps the run open on any server, also when its process died just after the turn before it ended. A follow-up whose answer stops at its length (max output tokens) is the run's answer, as its timeline shows it.
- A run cancelled while it works now reads `cancelled` as its first terminal status even when the turn the cancel cut puts it over its cap. Before, the server's check over the run's cost came ahead of the cancel: a cost that came in with the cut turn's end published `failed`, and the run then ended `cancelled`. A cancel the server took now decides the run's end before anything else does.
- A cancel means cancel: once a run is cancelled, its process sends no model request, however the cancel and the process's start interleave. serve marks the cancel in the run's directory before it reaches for the run's process, and the run's process refuses, before it is sent or counted, any request it would make after that mark. A cancelled run sends no compaction summary either, and a request it refused is not counted as one: the timeline skips pi's error entry for it (`REQUEST_REFUSED`). A request is checked against the cancel twice: once before its start is marked, and again after, right before it leaves. A cancel that lands while the mark is being written still stops the request, and its start is then withdrawn (`REQUEST_ENDED` with `unsent: true`), so the run's cost counts only what was sent.
- A run's cost includes pi's compaction summaries. A summary is a paid model request that pi books only in its own usage document, never as an entry the run's timeline sums, so a run that compacted under-counted its cost and its requests, and /usage with it. serve now marks a summary's request like a generation's (`request: "compaction"`) and writes its cost (`REQUEST_COST`, `agentrun.request_cost`), which the timeline counts and sums; one aborted before its answer, or open when the run settles, makes the cost unknown. A run that compacts also completes again: serve counted the messages still to arrive from the number of user rows the run showed, and a woken run's compacted conversation shows fewer, which read as messages still waiting, so the run stayed working; they are now counted by id.
- **A run's cost reads unknown once a model request was cut, never $0**
  - The timeline's cost row counts a request cut before its end (a stopped turn, a killed process) as unknown, so the run's sum reads "unknown" from then on. Before, the cut request added its stream's reported usage: $0 on the OpenAI Responses API, which reports usage only at the end, and only the input and first output token on Anthropic's Messages API. Either way the provider charged more than the sum showed.
  - serve's run cost takes the timeline's usage row as it is. A run opened again by a follow-up whose rows say unknown no longer shows the cost the agent store kept from its earlier end.
  - A request cut before it streamed any content leaves no assistant entry in the store, so it is not counted at all; that is unchanged here.
- A workflow's decision requests (its judge, pick, sift, route, ask and verify nodes on Jev or Parallel) count in its run metrics, so `/v2/stats` and Mission Control no longer undercount a workflow's tokens. `runMetricsWithDecisions(records, decisions)` takes the run's decision receipts beside its entries (`runMetricsOf(records)` keeps its one parameter, so `.map(runMetricsOf)` is safe): each is a request, by `provider/model`, with its `usage` (`input_tokens`, `output_tokens`) in the token sums; a receipt with no usage, or a cancelled one, is counted in `unmetered`, never as 0 tokens. serve passes a workflow run's receipts at its end.
- A pi run whose model ends its turn with a stop that holds only an empty text block (and perhaps an empty thinking block), with no tool call, now completes. Before, the timeline showed no row for it, so the run read `working` and `idle` until it was cancelled and its timeline never turned final. The timeline now shows a `final_answer` row with empty text for that answer, and the run completes with `result.final_answer` `""`. A message with no text block at all, such as a request the Claude or Codex bridge writes without content, still shows no row.
- A follow-up to a run that answered, or that the person stopped, no longer shows "Resumed after the server stopped" in the run's timeline. Once a run answers, its server lets its process go, and the process recorded that the same way as a server stop, so the next open read it as one. The server now tells the run's process when it opens the run for such a follow-up, and that open writes no "resumed" row. A run a server stop or a crash cut still shows its row when it resumes.
- Checking a harness's binary against its pins no longer reads the whole binary into memory, and runs once per binary in a process. `verifyCodexBinary` (the codex binary, 294 MiB) and `verifyClaudeAgentCli` (claude-agent's CLI, 231 MiB) each read the file whole to hash it, and agentrun-serve checked each again for every model it resolved: twice per harness in its start's probe, then once per save and once per run check, and a run's own process once more when it made its adapter. One start probe peaked at 395 MiB of memory, 675 MiB with a default model, past a 512 MiB container's limit. The hash now reads 1 MiB at a time, and a check that passed is kept for the process while the binary stays the same file (its path, device, inode, size, and modification and change times, with the pins); touched or replaced, it is checked again, and a failed check is never kept. The kernel's `@parcha/agentrun-kernel/host` exports both parts, `sha256OfFile` and `checkedOnce`.
- A claude-agent or codex run works in the run's workspace, the directory its file tools write to. Before, the CLI's working directory was an empty one beside it (`runs/<id>/harness/work`): Claude told its model that path, so a file written at its absolute path was refused as "outside the workspace", and the answer quoted the server's data directory. In QA this happened in 3 of 4 Claude runs that wrote a file.
  - `SessionIO.cwd`: a host may name the session's working directory, its workspace. The Claude and Codex adapters run their CLI there, and keep their own state in the session's private directory as before. git stops at the workspace's parent. serve passes the run's `work/`.
  - A file tool's refusal, an error such as ENOENT, and a write's or edit's result on claude-agent and codex name paths in the workspace relative to it (`notes/a.md`, `../escape.md: outside the workspace`), never by the server's data directory. A read's result is the file's own text and is not changed.
- A host tool's result reaches the model whole on claude-agent and codex.
