The Claude Agent CLI saved an MCP tool's result past 50,000 characters (a long `web_fetch` page, a `monid` answer, a 50 KB `read`) to a file under its own config directory and told the model to read it, which the run's confinement refuses, so the model could not read the page; Codex kept only the two ends of a result past its truncation policy (10,000 tokens for the models its catalog holds, about 12,000 characters for another). `@parcha/agentrun-kernel` states the bound once, `HOST_RESULT_MAX_CHARS` (120,000 characters, over every host tool's largest result); the Claude Agent adapter lists each host tool with it as `anthropic/maxResultSizeChars` and sets `MAX_MCP_OUTPUT_TOKENS` to it, and the Codex adapter sets `tool_output_token_limit` to it as Codex counts it (four bytes of UTF-8 a token: 90,000).
  `web_fetch` (and `fetch_page`) reads a page a part at a time: `offset` and `max_chars` (20,000 by default, at least 200, at most 50,000; a smaller one or an offset below 0 is refused before anything is fetched, saying so; a workflow's call node reads 50,000 by default) name the part, the Page gives `chars`, its length, and a part that is not the whole page has `truncated` while more follows, `next_offset` where it begins and `original_chars` where the whole length is known; an agent reads a line after the JSON saying which characters these are and the offset that reads on. A page whose HTML pi-browser cut at 1,000,000 characters before reading it gives `html_chars`, its whole length, on every part and no `original_chars`, and the line after its last part says `The page was cut at 1,000,000 characters of HTML before reading; the rest wasn't read.`; `converted: "text"` says its text is plain text, its conversion to markdown having run out of time. The text was cut at 60,000 characters with no way to read the rest. A page's links come with the part at offset 0, at most 200 in 20,000 characters, and a URL longer than 4,096 characters is refused before anything is sent.
  `web_search` leaves out its least relevant results when its answer would pass 100,000 characters, and `omitted` counts them.
- A learned rule that changes what an agent trusts or skips now always waits for its owner's OK, whichever model grades it. Before, the hold rested on the grader's reading alone, and models read the same rule differently: "refunds up to $250 need no manager" was held by one grading model and used at once under another. The end-of-run turn now marks such a note `risky: true`, and a note is held when either the run's turn or the grader says it is risky. The grader reads each note without that mark, so it still judges the note on its own (a planted instruction is still dropped). The mark is never kept: a held note says so by its status. The grader's rubric also names a limit or threshold under which someone may go ahead alone as risky, however it is phrased.
- A workflow's `llm` call is paid once and its tokens count. Each call completes on the run's marked models, so its cost is already a `REQUEST_COST` entry the run's cost sums, and serve added it again from the node log. The run's cost, the cost its end stored, its graph's total, `/v2/stats` and Mission Control all showed it twice; now each counts it once, and the cap, which already counted it once, is unchanged. That entry now also carries the completion's tokens as pi counts them (`input`, `output`, `cacheRead`, `cacheWrite`; none when pi counted none), so an `llm` call's tokens and a compaction summary's are in the run's metrics, once, rather than unmetered.
- `GET /v2/settings/tool-keys` names, for a model key, every harness its key runs that this server could run, before the key is saved: OpenRouter's row lists pi, claude-agent and codex, Anthropic's pi and claude-agent, OpenAI's pi and codex. Before, a harness waiting on a model key dropped out, so with none saved OpenRouter's row read pi alone. A harness left out is one this server cannot run for another reason: not installed, not the pinned build, or more than this host's memory. Where the host names claude-agent's endpoint (`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`), OpenRouter's row leaves claude-agent out, since that endpoint takes Anthropic's key alone. An `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` claude-agent refuses is now its reason before a missing key, and no key row lists claude-agent behind it. Capabilities' `harnesses` are unchanged.
- On a server started with `npx @parcha/agentrun`, a harness that is not set up (claude-agent without the Claude Agent SDK, codex without Codex) now says the one command that starts the same AgentRun again with it, on the same data: `npx -p @parcha/agentrun@<version> -p @parcha/agentrun-serve@<version> -p @anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk@0.3.286 agentrun --data ~/.agentrun` (with the store's absolute path). It keeps any other harness package already there, so setting up one never takes the other away. A server started with `npx @parcha/agentrun-serve` says the same with `agentrun-serve`, and adds `--data` only when its data is not in its default place. `npx @parcha/agentrun` keeps its data in `~/.agentrun` and hands it to the server, `import` and `reset-password` as `--data`, and its hint names that store as an absolute path, so the pasted line opens the same store from any directory; `agentrun-serve`'s own default, `~/.local/share/agentrun/agents`, does not change. When the server can't be downloaded, the command says so ("Couldn't download the AgentRun server (@parcha/agentrun-serve: not on npm). Your network or the release is the problem; nothing was started.") and exits 1, instead of saying the server did not start on a port.
- A pi run's model request is counted when it leaves, not when its answer starts. serve marks each generation request on the run's root before sending it (`agentrun.request_started`), and again if its stream ends aborted (`agentrun.request_ended`, with the provider's `responseId` when it named one before the cut). The timeline (`REQUEST_STARTED`, `REQUEST_ENDED` from `@parcha/pi-durable-agent/timeline`) counts every marked request: one with no `pi.assistant` entry (cut before its first content, by a stop, a cancel or a crash), other than the latest still in flight while the run works, makes the run's cost unknown (once the run is settled, a last request with no entry was cut by the process that ended), with every request counted, never the sum of the others. A run's rows are as before otherwise: no usage row appears for a request still in flight, and one with no marks reads as it did. A request the cap refuses is never sent and never marked; pi's compaction is not marked. A model stream that throws while it is read ends the request as an error instead of leaving the turn waiting.
- A process that exited and was never reaped (a zombie, as an orphan becomes under a PID 1 that does not reap, such as a container started without an init) is no longer counted as running. `processAlive(pid)` (from `@parcha/agentrun-kernel/host`) reads the process's state on Linux and falls back to `kill(pid, 0)` elsewhere; serve uses it to watch a run's process, so a server that restarts after its run's process died relaunches the run instead of waiting on the zombie forever.
- A claude-agent or codex run that a stop or a crash cut off mid-tool, and that the next process resumed, completes once it answers. The cut call's "interrupted" row used to be made at the end of the timeline, after the answer, when the next process's turn ended, and the server reads a run's last row to know it answered: the run stayed Working until another restart. The row now sits right after its call, in the live timeline and in the stored one.
- A message sent while a server ends a stop that the server before it saved is no longer lost. That server died after saving the stop and before its abort reached the run, so the turn still ran. The next server aborts the turn once it sees it, and the run reads stopped (`input_required`) at once. A follow-up sent in that window could reach the run before the abort. pi then withdrew it with the turn, so the message was accepted as a follow-up and never ran, and the run read `working` from then on. A message now waits for that abort as it waits for a stop in flight, then starts the run's next turn.
- A run's Docker box drops every capability, and a Docker daemon on another machine never binds this machine's `work/`.
  - `dockerBoxes({ dropCapabilities: true })` runs each box with `--cap-drop ALL` and `--security-opt no-new-privileges`. Serve's run boxes always use it. Without it, a server run as uid 0 made boxes that were root with Docker's default capabilities, so `bash` could `mknod` a device node in `work/` (`c 10 130`, the watchdog) for the file tools on the host to open. With it, `attach` ends a box made without it (an older version's, its run resumed) instead of taking it, so a fresh hardened box replaces it. The option is off by default in pi-transport, because a launcher that drops from root to another user needs Docker's default set.
  - `dockerBoxes`' `mounts` is now false when `DOCKER_HOST`, or the docker context (`DOCKER_CONTEXT`, else the config's `currentContext`), names a daemon on another machine (tcp://, ssh://, or a context whose record names no unix socket). With `DOCKER_HOST` and `DOCKER_CONTEXT` both set, both must be this machine, and each create checks again, so a `docker context use` after the provider was made refuses the next spec with mounts. A spec with mounts is then refused as `unsupported`, naming the setting and how to undo it. In serve, a run's box on such a daemon is not bound, so `read`, `write` and `edit` go through the box, never this machine's `work/`.
- A server killed or stopped while it builds or pulls the Docker box's image no longer leaves a run waiting on it for ten minutes and then failing with Docker's own error. The image's record names the server that wrote it (its pid and start time, as the box sweep checks a box record's writer). A run whose first box finds that server gone fails its call at once: `its image … is not ready: the server stopped while building it: restart the server to build it again`. The next start builds it again, as before.
  - A build or pull that fails is tried once more, 5 seconds later, by the same server. Meanwhile the box stays `preparing`, its `reason` saying what failed (`trying once more: <the failure in words>`). One that fails again is `unavailable` with the reason, as before. One that ran out of time is not tried again.
  - The serve README's recipe says `out.txt` names the image's Debian, with no version.
- A pi run with a spend cap no longer reads back more than it can have spent after its process restarts, which could refuse its next request with `spend_cap_reached` though the cap had room. A request a crash cut counts at the worst case its start mark holds. The spend read back took the largest such figures in the whole run, including requests an answer or a stop had already closed: a request answered at $0.20 (held $0.30) followed by one cut (held $0.10) read as $0.50, not $0.30. The marks are now read in order. Each request still open counts at its own start's figure. Where requests overlap and an answer could have closed either, it counts at the larger, so the spend is never read as less than what can have been billed.
  - An older start with no figure that an answer closed before the cut request started no longer makes the spend unknown.
  - pi's error for a request whose start mark could not be written, which never left, no longer counts as answering another request in the spend read back. Before, it could close a request still in flight, so the spend after a crash could be read as less than what that request can have cost.
- Under a spend cap, a model request whose response had begun and that then ended on an error counts at the worst case the cap held for it, as a request stopped before its end does. Before, it counted at its own usage, which is what had streamed when the error came (an overloaded event or a reset part way through: the input, and little or none of the output the provider can have billed), so a run could pass `max_cost_usd`. This holds in the run's process, where the next request is judged against the hold, and after a crash: the request's end mark says it ended on an error and holds the figure, and a summary that failed after it began writes its cost mark with `failed` and the hold. An error before any event of the response (refused at the HTTP level: a 429, a 5xx, which the provider does not bill and pi retries) still counts at its own usage, normally $0, and a request that never left (refused by the cap, its mark not written, a cancelled run's) counts nothing. The run's shown cost is unchanged.
- Under a `max_cost_usd`, a `monid.run` under way holds what it is held at (the price an inspect quoted, else the $1 allowance) until its charge is in, and a model request checked meanwhile counts it beside what the run has spent, on pi and on claude-agent and codex. Before this, a model request sent while a paid call was out was checked against the spend alone, so the two together could pass the cap. A paid call that fails without an answer counts at its held figure in the run's process, as its unsettled mark already counts once that process is gone.
- Under a `max_cost_usd`, a `monid.run` that may have charged and says no cost (stopped by the run's stop, past its wait, `TIMED_OUT`, `FAILED`, or one whose sending failed) now counts at what it was held at (the price an inspect quoted, else the $1 allowance), the same bound the cap let it go at. Before this it made the run's spend unknown, so every later model request and paid call was refused with `spend_cap_reached`, in the run's process and in one opened again from its store. A capped run whose monid call is stopped or times out now goes on while its cap allows, on pi, claude-agent and codex alike. Its cost still reads unknown (`null`), and `/usage`'s `tools` meter does too: the hold is the cap's bound, never a charge.
- A pi run's spend cap refuses once what the run has spent is unknown, as claude-agent's and codex's already did, prices a request stopped mid-answer at the most it can have cost, and refuses a model whose output is priced but that names no output ceiling on every harness.
  - The spend a pi run's process reads back from its store at start follows the run's own timeline cost, so every rule the timeline keeps for an unknown cost holds: a cost that is not a number, a cost still pending, a request marked as sent with no answer and no figure for it. Before, each of these counted as $0, and a capped run kept spending past what it could count. Now every later request is refused with `spend_cap_reached`, and so is every request after an answer in the same process whose cost is unknown.
  - A request stopped before its end (`POST /runs/{id}/stop`, or a summary that failed) counts at the worst case the cap held for it, never at the cut stream's own usage, which is not what the provider charged (the Responses API reports none until the end). Its end mark (`agentrun.request_ended`) carries that figure as `heldUsd`, so a later process counts it the same. A capped pi run that is stopped and sent another message goes on while its cap allows, and is refused with the cap's message once it does not. A request a crash cut (its process gone mid-request, so no answer and no end) counts the same way, from the figure its start mark (`agentrun.request_started`) carries as `heldUsd`: a capped pi run opened again after its process died goes on while its cap allows. A stopped or cut request from a store written before this release has no figure and still reads unknown. The run's shown cost is unchanged: a stopped or cut request shows unknown.
  - A request the cap refuses gets a start mark of its own (`agentrun.request_started` with `capRefused: true`) before pi writes its error entry, so that entry no longer stands for an earlier request a stop or a crash cut, and the run's cost shows unknown there, never $0.
  - A model with no output ceiling (no max output tokens and no context window) was priced at no output, or at a worst case that was not a number and passed every check. Under a cap it is now refused with `spend_cap_reached`, unless its output costs nothing at every price tier, which keeps a known worst case. No model in pi-ai's catalog, and no gateway model serve builds, is affected.
  - The README's "Spend cap" section says what the cap does on every harness: the input bound (UTF-8 bytes plus what the provider adds, not pi-ai's estimate), a model listed at $0 as a known $0, and what is refused under a cap.
- claude-agent runs on a model named the way OpenRouter names it. A revision naming `anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5` sent Anthropic the bare dotted `claude-sonnet-5.5`, an id neither Anthropic nor OpenRouter knows, and its runs failed. claude-agent now sends Anthropic's own id, the model id with its dots as dashes (`claude-sonnet-5-5`), which Anthropic takes and OpenRouter takes too, and prices the run by the same id, so its cost is the catalog's estimate rather than unknown. The run's `max_cost_usd` reads the same id, so a capped run named the dotted way is held to its cap like any other. The doubled `anthropic/anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5`, once the workaround, is now refused with `422` on `definition.model`, naming the form to use: `anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5`. The `LOOPBACK` allow-list carries the key gate's loopback marker.
- When claude-agent can't run here, its reason now names the missing Claude Agent SDK, with its install line, before any missing key. A server with no SDK and no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` used to say only that the key was missing. Setting the key would then not have made claude-agent available, and the image's smoke check failed on that reason. The key is still named once the SDK is in place.
- A server stopped while setup is still counting this machine's sessions now exits at once. Before, its close waited for the count's worker to finish listing every session file, because the listing never checks for a stop. On a home with many sessions that held the exit for seconds, past the drain window. The count writes nothing, so close now ends its worker and waits for it to exit, and the next start counts again. An import under way still stops between two session files and is waited for, so nothing writes the store after close.
- Every agentic run's system prompt now starts with one line that says today's date and where the run works: "Today is 2026-11-03 (UTC). You are running on AgentRun; your workspace is your working directory, and file paths are relative to it." pi, claude-agent and codex get the same line. Before, pi was never told the date, so a question about the latest release was answered from the date its model was trained on ("As of March 8, 2025, ..."). The line is made again as each turn starts: on pi it is a prompt section rendered before every model request, so a run resumed on a later day is told that day; claude-agent and codex get it in the session's instructions each time the run's process opens the session.
- The decision keys are held like the server's other secrets. `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` and `PARALLEL_API_KEY` are on the launcher's list, so started by its launcher the server gets them over the secrets pipe and holds them out of its own environment. A workflow run's process gets a decision key on its decision descriptor alone, never with the secrets it is handed over its own pipe, which are its tools' environment. The server chooses Jev's key from the secrets it holds, so a key the launcher handed over, the OpenRouter gateway's included, still reaches the run's judge.
- A workflow's typed questions honour the node's own decision settings, and a decision's unknown cost survives a restart.
  - A run's judge goes through `judgeFromProviders` over the server's providers (Jev here), and the run's checks before any node runs take the same providers. A workflow naming a provider the server does not run is refused at save, `422` on `definition.workflow`, naming the provider, instead of being asked of Jev; a node asking what its provider cannot give (evidence required of Jev, which gives none) is refused before the run, naming the node. A node's `timeoutMs` bounds its request, and each receipt names the provider that answered.
  - A workflow run a restarted server adopts as ended reads its decisions' cost from its store again, so a run with a decision of unknown cost reads `cost.usd: null`, never the model's priced usage alone.
- A workflow run's decision rows are now cleaned and cut like every other timeline row. Each URL in a row's summary or text keeps its origin, path and query keys and loses its query values, fragment and credentials. The summary is cut at 500 characters and the text at 2,000. A decision stored before receipts kept their status now reads as answered or failed from its rule and error instead of `undefined`, and an old route names the branch it took. When a run's decisions can't be read, its timeline's usage row reads unknown instead of showing the model nodes' cost alone. `GET /v2/openapi.json` now lists `decision` in `TimelineKind`, marked `decision-rows/1`, so a client that validates rows against it accepts them.
- `PUT /v2/agents/{agent_id}/definition` takes the contract's body: the definition itself, as hosted takes it, with the contract's preconditions. `If-Match` is required (`412 precondition_failed` without it), and `definition.version` must be greater than the current one (`422` on `definition.version`). Any greater version is taken and always makes a new revision, the same definition saved again included. This server numbers its revisions in order, so the answer's `definition.version` is the next number, whatever larger one the body named. A client written to the contract can now edit an agent here. This server's own body, `{definition, skills?, note?}` like `POST /agents`, still works with `If-Match` optional, and the served document now describes both bodies as exactly one of the two, marked `definition-body/1`, plus the `note` that `POST /agents` takes.
- `GET /v2/openapi.json` now says which definition fields serve takes, and what a run it refuses holds. `DefinitionInput` showed the contract's `input_schema`, `output_schema` and `max_cost_usd` as fields, though serve refuses each with `422 not_allowed`. Each is now marked `x-agentrun-serve-unsupported` with the reason its 422 gives, and listed in `info.x-agentrun-serve.unsupported_fields`. `ErrorBody.details` gains `x-agentrun-serve-details`: the shape of `agent_not_runnable`'s details here: what keeps the revision from running (`unbound`, `missing`, `harness`, and for a workflow `adapters`, `code`, `tier`), or, for an archived agent, `archived_at`. `harness`, `model` and `thinking` were served already, as `x-agentrun-serve` additions.
- An edit sent without `If-Match` that another edit overtakes while it is being checked is built and checked again on the new current revision. What it leaves out (skill files, harness, place) now comes from the revision it is committed on, never from the one it first read, so it cannot undo the other edit.
- serve's test hooks (`AGENTRUN_SERVE_FAUX`, `AGENTRUN_SERVE_FAUX_HARNESS` and every name after that prefix) are set from `startServe`'s options alone. A name in the server's own environment is dropped from what the server reads and from every run's process. Before, a published server honoured these names from its environment with no option: a leftover export gave every run fake answers or loaded a module by path.
- `fetch_page` now runs on `claude-agent` and `codex`, not only on pi. A revision naming it on either harness used to save with a warning that it was not bound yet, and every run of it was refused `409 agent_not_runnable`, so those agents could not read the web here. It is bound as the file tools are: pi's own tool, public-host guard included, may run again after a crash since it only reads. Capabilities' `tools[].harnesses` now names the harnesses that run each tool here (`x-agentrun-serve: tool-harness/1`), so a client can offer exactly the tools the chosen harness runs.
- **A completed run takes a follow-up, as the contract says**
  - `POST /runs/{id}/messages` to a completed agentic run is a `follow_up`: the run opens again on its own store and session, under its spend cap, answers, and completes again. It used to be `409 not_continuable`. It is the same run, before and after a server restart.
  - A follow-up sent while the run's end is still being written (its process closing, or its carry back waiting on a busy store) waits for it, up to a request's deadline, then continues the run; past the deadline it is `503 unavailable`, to try again.
  - Opening it takes a place under `--max-runs`: with none free the message is `429 rate_limited`, and the run stays completed.
  - A failed or cancelled run, an archived run and a run of an archived agent stay `409 not_continuable`: archiving closes a run.
  - The agent store has `reopenRun`, which opens a completed run again (running, no end); its cost and summary stay until the next `carryBack`.
- On codex and claude-agent, a request's cost is written with its answer even when it is booked after the harness has the answer: the harness's proxy answers before it books the response. A turn's end now waits up to 2 s for those costs. A cost that comes after the wait leaves the answer written with its cost pending (`REQUEST_COST_PENDING`): the run's cost reads unknown until it comes in, as a `REQUEST_COST_LATE` the timeline adds to the cost without counting another request. So the run's spend, its spend cap and `/usage` count it once, and one paid request is one request. If the process goes before it can, the next open writes it from the session's booking, and never writes the request twice. Through a gateway (`AGENT_MODEL_BASE_URL`), codex now sends the model id as the gateway was configured with it, provider-qualified or not, and no longer strips `openai/` from it. On pi, a run's spend cap now also counts its compaction summaries' cost, read back from its store when its process starts, which it did not count before: a run near its cap can be refused a request it used to be allowed.
- A cancel holds on every harness. A run on codex or claude-agent whose cancel serve has marked sends no further model request, as a pi run already did. Each request the harness's session asks for is refused before it leaves: the turn ends on the refusal, with nothing sent or paid. That holds whatever started the turn, a message or a recovery the run's process starts itself. A cancel that lands after a request's check and before its record is written is caught too: the booking reads the cancel again at both points. A request withdrawn that way is not counted when the run opens again: no answer is written for it, and it costs nothing.
- A codex or claude-agent run's model proxy now reports to the run's log. serve gave neither adapter a log, so the proxy's lines were dropped: a refused request, a response whose ids could not be written, a usage or a price that could not be read. Each is now one JSON line in the run's `process.log` (`event: "harness"`, with the harness's id). A request's path is written without its query string, and every value of the environment's key-shaped variables (`*_API_KEY`, `*_TOKEN`, `*SECRET*`) is cut out of the line, so a key a caller sent, or one serve holds, never reaches the log. A failed run's message is never taken from such a line: it comes from the process's own last line before them.
- A run on codex or claude-agent marks each model request on its root as the request leaves, as a pi run's are. A run that ends with a request still out and never opens again then carries back a cost that reads unknown, never the sum of the others without it. That is a run cancelled, or failed, mid-request.
- An unavailable `claude-agent` or `codex` now says when a restart is part of the fix. The server checks each harness's package and pins once, at its start, so a harness installed after it, or put back on its pinned build, stays unavailable until AgentRun restarts. Its reason in `GET /v2/capabilities` (and in a save's warning or a run's `409`) now ends with `once it is installed, restart AgentRun to pick it up`, or `once the pinned build is installed, restart AgentRun`. Run from npx, a reason whose install line is already the npx line that restarts the server with what is missing is unchanged, as is a reason a setting fixes, such as a missing model key.
- An agent's home matches its host-owned names by identity: a path's first segment is compared folded (compatibility form, any case, no combining marks) and, where it and `learning/` are both on disk, by device and inode. Home paths take only plain segments, each its own NFC form with no default-ignorable code point; any other is `400 invalid_file_path`. The home path checks are in their own module, `home-path.ts`.
- A host tool's long output (another harness's `bash`, `read` and the rest) is cut at a character boundary. A cut that fell inside a multi-byte UTF-8 character used to show as `�` at the start of a kept tail or the end of a kept head; the character is now left out whole, and the text stays within the tool's byte limit. A character whose bytes arrive in two output chunks is decoded as one.
- AgentRun Server's image carries no Claude Agent SDK until its licence is reviewed for the image. The SDK (with its platform packages, which hold the Claude CLI) is a dev dependency of the Claude Agent adapter, so the image's `npm ci` installed it. The Dockerfile now removes it after the build, and `docker/image-check.sh` fails the build if any copy is left in the final tree, nested ones and a bin link included. Until then the image reports claude-agent as not installed, with the line that installs it. The Codex CLI is not held back.
  - `docker/image-smoke.sh <image> [docker run arguments...]` checks that a built image serves. In a container published on a loopback port only, `GET /v2/health` answers 200 ok and `GET /v2/capabilities` answers with the contract. Where capabilities list harnesses, pi is available; claude-agent is never available: it is unavailable as not installed, with its install line, or as a harness this server does not run yet; and any other harness is available, or unavailable with a reason a person can act on, not an import error. On a failure it shows the container's log with keys withheld, and it always removes the container.
- `agentrun-serve import` imports sessions from current Codex versions. These write the person's turns only as user messages, with no `user_message` event, so the importer found no message of the person's and skipped every such session unless `--all` was passed. The person's turns now come from the session's `user_message` events when it has any, as older versions wrote both, and otherwise from its user messages, so each turn is counted once. A user message's blocks that the CLI adds are left out: its own elements such as `<environment_context>`, an AGENTS.md heading over `<INSTRUCTIONS>`, and the tags around an image. A prompt the person wrote as a tagged element stays theirs.
- `agentrun-serve import` cuts the server's own keys (the launcher's secrets, such as `OPENAI_API_KEY`) out of what it keeps. The server's start moves those keys out of its environment, so the import's cut never saw them, and a session that held one kept it in the store. They are now read back in for the cut and replaced with `[redacted]` wherever they appear. A session's id is cut too: one named after its file, when the file's name held a key, no longer keeps it, and a later import still finds the run it made.
- An imported run's page and its stored metrics give one number, and every subagent request counts once. Each of a run's subagents' requests is written into its transcript as a request with no answer of its own there, its usage and cost with it, so the timeline's usage row, the run's cost and its metrics count the same requests and tokens; the timeline shows no extra row. A session written in several files under one id (a Codex thread resumed later, a Claude Code session resumed in another directory and copied into that project's folder) is one run, its files merged in order through the same record count; the import used to keep only the first file while the dry run counted them all. Each subagent's file counts once: a Codex child in its thread's run (it used to count once per file of the thread), a Claude Code subagent with the session file beside its folder. A record a file copies moves no time at work, whatever its type (a copied system or meta record used to add its gaps again), and a file holding only copies adds nothing to its session. The import reads a project's files in one order, Claude Code's by folder and oldest written first, so the first file holding a copied record keeps it whichever way the import is run. A session's files sit in the project of its first file by start. A subagent tied to no session brings its own subagents' usage into the summary's totals. Merged, a session's cost is counted once (Claude Code writes a running total, which a moved copy carries on), its turns follow the order its files ran, and its state and answer are those of the file that ended last. A subagent's failed request keeps its known zero tokens: a `REQUEST_COST` mark with `failed: true` has its counts taken as they are, as a failed answer's are.
- An import (`agentrun-serve import` and setup's import) now cuts the secrets this server made and keeps out of everything it writes and prints, by their exact values: its API token (`ar_local_...`), its door token, its sign-in secret, and its webhook's signing secret (`whsec_...`) when the seal key opens it. Before, one pasted into a session was imported as it was. Each counts once in "N secrets removed", as the other keys do. Still imported as they are: a key-shaped value this server did not make (a `whsec_` secret from another service) and a run's own door token.
- `agentrun-serve import` (and its `--dry-run`) now says once, on stderr, when it cannot open this server's webhook signing secret to cut it: with no `AGENTRUN_SEAL_KEY`, or one the secret was not sealed under, it prints `event=webhook_secret condition=seal_key_missing` (or `condition=unreadable`) `name=AGENTRUN_SEAL_KEY`, the file, and that the import does not cut it, in the same log-line form as the tool keys' `seal_key_missing` line. The import goes on as before. Before, it left the secret uncut and said nothing. What the CLI says on stderr (this line, and the tool keys' line) now goes through the same cut as its printed lines, so a key in the data directory's path prints as `[redacted]`.
- A `PUT /v2/settings/tools`, and a tool key's `PUT` or `DELETE /v2/settings/tool-keys/{id}`, answers in milliseconds: each one used to check every harness again (each CLI's package and pins, codex's binary hashed), which took seconds, though neither a tool setting nor a tool key changes what a harness offers. The server checks the harnesses once at its start and remakes only what a write changes, so capabilities, a save's checks and a run's checks read right after the answer show it: what a web tool resolves to, a tool's `available` and `missing`, the decision providers. A start logs the check's length as `event=harness_offers why=start ms=<n>`. A save or a run check no longer checks the harnesses again after a key write either.
- A claude-agent or codex run's cost no longer reads unknown until the run is opened again when bookings come in late or out of order. The proxy books a response when its stream closes, which can be after the harness has its answer, after its turn has ended, and after a later request's booking.
  - A late cost for a request written at unknown cost goes on the root as soon as it comes in, wherever it sits among the costs still to write. Before, it was written only when it was the oldest of them, so behind a later request's cost it waited for the next open.
  - A cost that comes in with no turn running is written then. Before, a request still unbooked at its turn's end, whose cost came only after the turn, had no root entry until the next open.
  Each request is still written once, and each cost once. The cap reads the session's own bookings, as before.
- A run whose launch takes the server down no longer takes it down again at every start. serve writes a launch mark in the run's directory before it spawns the run's process, and removes it once that process serves or exits. A start that finds the mark fails the run once, with `run_failed` and `error.details.reason` `server_lost_at_launch`, instead of launching it again. Before, a spawn that aborted serve itself (as on macOS) was repeated by the next start's recovery, so serve went down at every start until the operator removed the run. `RunError.details` is this server's own field, marked `x-agentrun-serve` in the served OpenAPI document; the vendored contract is unchanged. A run whose process came up and then died still opens again as before. If the mark cannot be removed (a read-only or failing disk), serve logs `launch_mark_kept` with the run and keeps serving; the mark left behind fails that run at the next start.
- An `llm` call whose tier the state fills in (`tier: "{requestedTier}"`) now runs on the model of the tier it asks for. Before, the run registered a model only for tiers written literally, so such a call asking for `strong` or `fast` was answered by the default model (`AGENT_MODEL`) without a word, and billed at its price. A templated tier now registers all three tiers, and their models are checked before the run as a literal tier's is; and a call naming a tier the run has no model for fails with `llm: tier <tier> has no model in this run` instead of falling back. A call that names no tier still runs on the default model.
- `POST /v2/runs/{id}/messages` no longer answers `409 home_busy` for a message it has already taken. Its answer read the run's stored row after the message went in. So while another connection held the agent store, a message that ran was answered `home_busy`, and a client that retried sent it twice. The answer's `status` and `state` now come from the run itself. `POST /v2/agents/{id}/runs` had the same shape: its answer read the stored row after the run was written and its first message counted. A busy store answered `409 home_busy` for a run that ran, and a retry without an Idempotency-Key started a second one. A run's start now answers from the run itself, and so do a stop and a cancel, which read the store after they took effect only for their status.
- A message sent while a stop is in flight is no longer lost. `POST /v2/runs/{id}/stop` sets its mark, then saves it and aborts the turn through the run's process. A message that came in that gap read the run stopped, went in as a follow-up to the turn the abort was cutting, and was dropped with it: pi withdraws the inputs it had queued, and a harness door hook drops its own queue. Its row never came, so the run read `working` for good and kept its place under `--max-runs` until a cancel or a restart. A message now waits, up to a request's deadline, for a stop in flight to answer, including one that began while the message itself was waiting (on the run's end, its reopening, or the agent store), then goes in as a follow-up to the stopped run and starts its next turn. `startServe` takes `beforeAbort`, a test's seam awaited by a stop between its mark and its abort.
- A claude-agent or codex run whose model asks for two or more tools in one answer completes. A harness reports each call as it runs it, and serve wrote the answer at its first call's end, so each later call of that answer went into the next answer's entry, after its own result: the closing answer then held a tool call, the call read as interrupted though it had succeeded, and the run stayed `working` until it was cancelled. Such a call is now written as a continuation of its answer (`agentrun.request_continued`), before its result. The timeline shows it under the answer it continues, and counts it as a tool call but not as a model request.
- A run's process no longer registers pi's `bash` tool. Every run now installs pi's `read`, `write` and `edit` without `bash`, and a run on another harness takes its host file tools from the same set. Models were never offered `bash` here; now the run's process does not hold it at all.
- A missing Codex now names the line that fixes it for how the server was started, as claude-agent's SDK does. Run from npx, its reason gives `npx -p @parcha/agentrun-serve@<this version> -p @openai/codex@0.164.0 agentrun-serve`; installed in a project, the `npm install --save-exact @openai/codex@0.164.0` line, as before.
- claude-agent's missing Agent SDK names the line that fixes it for how the server was started. Run from npx (`npx @parcha/agentrun-serve`), where there is no project to install into, its reason in `GET /v2/capabilities` and in a refused run gives `npx -p @parcha/agentrun-serve@<this version> -p @anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk@0.3.286 agentrun-serve`, which runs the same server with the SDK beside it. Installed in a project, it gives the `npm install --save-exact …` line, as before. The server tells npx by where its own files are: under npm's `_npx` cache.
- A refused read, write or edit of a path outside the workspace no longer names a host path. pi's file tools resolve a path against the workspace before the confinement checks it, so the refusal named the resolved path (`../../.token` read as `<data>/runs/.token`), the workspace's own directory, and, through a link, where the link points. `PathOutsideWorkError`'s message now names no path (`outside the workspace`); a caller tells a refusal from any other error by its type and its `reason`. agentrun serve names the refusal by the path the call gave, as it gave it (`../../.token: outside the workspace`), on pi and on the other harnesses alike, on the runner and in a box (where the refusal named the box's work dir too), so neither the model nor the run's timeline reads the server's data directory.
- Parallel's fees for `web_search` and `web_fetch` are now the run's spend. Each search ($0.001 for up to 10 results,
  $0.001 for each one past them) and each page Parallel extracts ($0.001 a URL), at Parallel's published price, is booked
  as a tool cost (`agentrun.tool_cost`, `charged_by: "parallel"`): a line of its own on the run's timeline, part of its
  cost and of `/usage`'s `tools` meter. Under a spend cap each is a paid call, sent only when it fits what the cap has
  left; past it a search answers the cap's refusal and a page is the guarded direct fetch, and nothing reaches Parallel.
  One sent holds its price until Parallel answers, and a model request checked meanwhile counts it beside the spend, so
  the two never pass the cap together. Before, these calls were billed by Parallel but counted nowhere, and a cap did not
  hold them.
- `pdf_pages` draws each document under a page directory of its own. Before, the directory was named by the document's file name alone, so `company-a/certificate.pdf` and `company-b/certificate.pdf` drawn in one run both answered `pages/certificate/<dpi>/1.png`, and the second draw replaced the first: a judge about company A could be shown company B's page. The directory is now the document's path below `--documents` (or the run's `work/`) made one path-safe name and ended with 12 hex characters of its hash, such as `pages/company-a_certificate-56c2fc556c26/110/1.png`; the layout `work/pages/<stem>/<dpi>/<n>.png` is unchanged, and the same file always draws under the same name. Pages a run drew before keep their old `pages/<name>/` directories, and refs to them still load; a new draw goes under the new name.
- A run that has ended (failed, completed, cancelled) gives its place back once its process exits, and a start that needs that place now waits for it, up to that process's drain and kill, instead of being refused `429 rate_limited` for a place already being given back. Under load, a failed run's process can outlive the moment the run reads failed; the next start no longer depends on which comes first. A completed run's follow-up waits the same way. Two follow-ups that reach a completed run while it waits for a place share its one reopening, and both are sent to the run; the second is no longer refused with 429 or `unavailable`.
- A model key set for one of pi-ai's other providers (`GEMINI_API_KEY`, `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY`, `XAI_API_KEY`, Bedrock's `AWS_*` keys, about forty in all) is now held like `OPENAI_API_KEY`: the launcher takes it out of the server's environment, and a run's process gets it over its pipe only when its model is that provider's. Every model key now goes only to the runs whose model needs it: a pi run its model's provider's (per tier for a workflow), or `AGENT_MODEL_API_KEY` through the gateway; a codex run `OPENAI_API_KEY`, or the gateway's; a claude-agent run `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. Before, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` and `AGENT_MODEL_API_KEY` went to every run. Before, the launcher named four providers' keys, and every other one stayed in the server's environment and in every run's, where Codex's process, a program a run started or any process of the same user could read it. Operators set the same variables.

  - No run's environment, nor docker's probe's, holds a variable named like a key, on the launcher's list or not: one of its `_`-separated segments, in any case, is `KEY`, `TOKEN`, `SECRET`, `PASSWORD`, `PASSWD`, `PASS`, `PAT`, `CREDENTIAL`, `CREDENTIALS` or `DSN`, or its last is `PWD` after another (never `PWD` or `OLDPWD`). It is pi-durable-disk's rule, and the import's cut and a harness's log take it too. A credential an SDK finds through such a variable (Bedrock's web identity token file or container role, `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS`, `ANTHROPIC_IDENTITY_TOKEN_FILE`) is not supported for runs.
  - A run on Bedrock passes the AWS keys its process holds to each request, as pi-ai's Bedrock request reads them, instead of from an environment.
  - The decision keys go only where a run needs them, still on their own descriptor: Jev's (by its route) to a workflow with a node that names Jev or none, Parallel's to one with a Parallel node or to a run that binds `web_fetch`, OpenRouter's to one with an OpenRouter node. A workflow with no decision node, like an agentic run without `web_fetch`, gets no descriptor; before, every workflow got every decision key the server held.
  - Not covered: a key-shaped variable the launcher's list does not name stays in the server's own environment; Bedrock credentials by container full URI with `AWS_CONTAINER_AUTHORIZATION_TOKEN` are not supported for runs; a pi run on a `typesafe/` model does not get `TYPESAFE_API_KEY`, a decision key.
- Every refusal and reason about places, boxes and the agent's home now names the next step.
  - A place's `422` on `definition.place` carries a `fix`: the place it most likely meant, to copy (`"place": {"mode": "sandbox", "box": "docker"}` for `"place": "docker"` or `{"mode": "docker"}`), or the field to remove. `bash` on a runner agent gets the sandbox place as its fix; the browser in a sandbox, `Remove "browser" from definition.tools.`; a workflow in a sandbox, `"place": {"mode": "runner"}` (an edit that leaves the place out keeps the agent's).
  - A box that `AGENTRUN_BOXES` does not list says `not enabled on this server: start AgentRun with AGENTRUN_BOXES=docker`, keeping the boxes the setting already lists. The same reason is in capabilities, a save's warning and a run's `409`.
  - A `docker` whose daemon does not answer says `docker version failed (exit 1): start Docker, or add this server's user to the docker group, then restart this server`. A missing CLI says to install Docker. A save's warning and a run's `409` end in one full stop (no more `?.`).
  - `capabilities.tools`' `bash` is `available: false`, with a `reason` naming each box's own, when no box this server has can run it.
  - The start banner adds a line saying which boxes are ready (`Boxes: docker is ready, so sandbox agents run their tools there.`), or why none is. `startServe` returns it as `boxesLine`.
  - A read under the home's `learning/` names `/v2/agents/{id}/learning` only on a server that has that route. Otherwise it says `the server keeps learning/ for itself; this server has no learning API yet`.
  - The browser's sandbox refusal is in plain words.
  - (`@parcha/pi-durable-agent`) A timeline row for a call answered only by the harness's diagnostics block (a tool that threw or is not offered) is summarized by its error (`Ran \`true\` failed: Tool bash is not available`), not by the block's `<harness>` tag.
- A server started on a data root whose server is still stopping now waits for that stop instead of refusing. A stop keeps the data root until its runs' processes have drained and exited, which takes up to the drain timeout and a second (31 s by default), and the API keeps answering, with runs `working`, meanwhile. A start in that window used to exit at once with "another server holds this data root: stop it". Now a stopping server prints `agentrun-serve: stopping: waiting up to N s for N runs to finish their turn` as its close begins, when runs' processes are left to finish, and records in `<data>/server.state` when its stop is over at the latest. A server writes that file once as it starts (its pid, running) and its stop rewrites it in place, so a stop adds no entry to the data root: a caller that removes the directory right after it signals the stop meets nothing new there. A start that finds the lock held reads that file, prints `agentrun-serve: the server on this data root (pid N) is stopping; waiting for it, up to N s`, takes the lock once the stop is over, and opens the runs the stop cut again. A start on a data root whose server runs is refused at once, as before, and so is one whose record names a process that is gone or a time that has passed. `startServe` takes `notice`, called with both lines in place of standard error. A server's `close()` runs once: a second call returns the first one's promise, and never rewrites the record of a server that started on the data root since.
- A run's timeline no longer loses rows when its end is carried back. The live "Run started" and "Run finished" rows, which no agent-store entry records on any harness, are never served, so the rows a reader pages through once a run reads `completed` are the rows it ends with. A cancel sent while a run that answered is finishing is refused (`cancel_requested: false`, `already_terminal: true`), and the run ends `completed` instead of `cancelled`.
- A run's files line quotes each name as a JSON string, with any control, format or line-separator character as its `\uXXXX` escape: `Files for this run: "inputs/notes.txt" (32 B).` A name holding `", ` could forge another entry, and an RTL override or a zero-width character could reorder what the model read. The line also reads only `work/`'s own `inputs/`, walked from `work/` as the run's file list is: a link at `inputs` names nothing from where it points, and the run starts as before.
- A run runs where its agent's definition puts it: a run request names no place, and `POST /agents/{id}/runs` with `place` is `422 input_invalid` at `place`, as with any field the body does not keep. The served OpenAPI document has no `RunCreate.place`, and the console's run bar and playground offer no "Run on the server instead".
- The server's secrets are out of every process's environment. `agentrun-serve`, the bin and the image's entrypoint, is now a small launcher. It hands the server each secret it names over a pipe and starts it with `env -u` for each, so none is in the server's `/proc/<pid>/environ` or command line. The secrets are the API token, `AGENT_MODEL_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `BROWSERBASE_API_KEY` and `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`. Before it starts any other process, the launcher moves each secret out of its environment into its own memory. The server reads the pipe first thing and closes it, and hands each run's process the run secrets over a pipe of its own, never its environment, which the run's process reads in a preload before anything else loads. In the image, PID 1 is still tini, started by the launcher. Capabilities' `secrets` says how secrets are held and the limit: in memory, they rely on the kernel refusing a process of the same user to attach (Yama `ptrace_scope` 1 or more). Operators still set the same variables. Started as `node dist/cli.js`, the server reads them from its environment, as before. The bin needs a POSIX `sh` with a builtin `printf`: Linux or macOS, not Windows.
- A data root serves one server at a time, and the server now enforces it instead of the README only saying so. A server takes `<data>/server.lock` (mode 0600) and holds it until it closes. It takes the lock after the checks that only read and refuse, such as agent homes from 0.1.0-beta.26, so a start they refuse still creates nothing. It takes it before anything that writes. A second server on the same root, in another process or in the same one, is refused before it opens a store, adopts a run or binds a port. The CLI exits 1 with one line: `agentrun-serve: another server holds this data root (<data>/server.lock): stop it, or start this one with another --data`. A crashed server's lock is released by the kernel, so the next start reuses the file. `takeServerLock(dataRoot)` and `ServerLockHeldError` are exported.
- **`/v2/stats` counts an escalated run as completed and needing review, never as failed**
  - An escalated workflow run is `completed` on `/v2/runs`, with a `review`, but `/v2/stats` counted it as failed: `runs.failed` added the escalated runs, and each analytics group counted them in `failed`. Now `runs.failed` counts failed runs alone, and `runs.needs_review` counts the escalated ones. Each analytics group (totals, by harness and effort, by day, by source) counts an escalated run in `completed`, with `needs_review` beside it, under the agent and date filters too. The new fields are marked `stats/3`.
  - The agent store's `AnalyticsGroup` gains `needsReview`, and its `completed` includes the escalated runs, which its `failed` no longer counts.
  - A client that showed `failed` as "failed or escalated" should show `needs_review` beside it.
- A capped run that the person stops can go on. A request you stop now counts what it streamed, plus up to 1,024 output tokens the provider may have produced after the last word we read, and never more than it was held. Before, it counted at its whole hold. Since each request is limited to what is left of the cap, that hold was the rest of the cap. So a $1 run stopped after $0.004 of spend read $1 spent, and its next message failed with `spend_cap_reached`.
  - **On pi:** a stopped request counts its input bound, each byte of text, thinking and tool calls it streamed as a token, and `STOP_ALLOWANCE_TOKENS` (1,024) of output, at the model's price, at most its hold. Its end mark (`agentrun.request_ended`) carries that figure as `heldUsd`, so a later process counts it the same.
  - **On `claude-agent` and `codex`:**
    - The model proxy reports a response its client left part way, its cost unknown, with the bytes it had sent (`stoppedAfterBytes`).
    - A runner `beforeRequest` may answer `stoppedUsd(streamedTokens)` beside `holdUsd`. The runner counts such a response at the lesser of the two and writes the figure on its `runner.turn` (`heldUsd`, `stopped: true`).
    - A response the provider ended early, or one cut by a crash, still counts at its hold.
  - **Unchanged:** a request that ended on an error after its response began, a failed summary and a crash cut still count at the hold. The run's shown cost is unchanged: a stopped request reads unknown, beside its cost so far.
- A stopped run stays stopped until the person sends another message, whatever happens to the messages it shows. The stop is kept as a place in the run's store (`stopped_at` in the run's `serve.json`: the stopped message's id, or place 0 when the stop came before any message), and the run reads stopped while no message shown is newer. Before, it was a count of the messages shown, which a compaction changes. Now a run stopped and then compacted stays stopped, even when the compaction summarized the stopped message itself. A run that was sent a new message and then compacted back to the stop's count no longer reads as stopped. A stopped run whose compaction summarized every message it showed reads `input_required`, not `queued`. A run with no rows shown yet still reads `queued`. A stop the run refuses leaves nothing saved. A run stopped by an older server (`stopped_after`, a count) reads as before.
- A stop works on a run that is working but still reads `queued`. A message sent to a run shows in its timeline only once the run's process reports it, so right after a start, on a loaded machine, a run well into its turn could still read `queued` (state `working`), and `POST /v2/runs/{id}/stop` answered `stopped: false`. The stop now waits, up to a request's deadline, until the run's timeline shows every message sent to it, then stops the turn as it stops any working run: `stopped: true`, and the run reads `input_required`. It waits for the timeline first so that what the stop records as the person's last message is the one the turn was answering, and the stop holds once that message shows. A run that ends, is cancelled or loses its process while the stop waits answers `stopped: false`, as before.
- A stopped run stays `input_required` and `idle` across a server restart. It used to read `working` and `idle`, and when its process was gone the next server opened it again as if it still had work to do. The server now keeps the stop in the run's `serve.json`, as the count of messages the person had sent when it was stopped, so the next message ends the stop with nothing to delete.
  A server that stopped between saving a stop and its abort reaching the run left the turn working while the run read stopped; the next server now finishes that stop, aborting the adopted turn once.
- **serve no longer stalls while another process holds the agent store**
  - Every read and write of the agent store waits for a busy store without blocking the server, so other requests are still answered meanwhile. Before, one write could hold up every request for up to 5 s.
  - A request waits up to about 2 s for the store, then answers `409 home_busy` with `retry_after_ms`. A run's end keeps trying until it is written.
  - Creating an agent while the store was busy answered `409 agent_exists`, and a read answered `500 internal`. Both now wait, then answer `home_busy` if the store stays busy.
  - An edit that waits for the store is checked against the revision current when it commits, so two edits held up together cannot both write: the second gets `422` at `definition.version`, or, being the same edit, the revision the first made.
  - A workflow run that ends while the store is held waits for it as long as a run's end does, and one still held after that is settled again every 30 s, not left until the next start.
  - A run's answer is read at one moment: a run that ends while its read waits answers ended, with its result.
- A pi run on a gateway model that has no price (an id pi-ai's catalog does not list, with no `AGENT_MODEL_PRICE` or tier price) now reads its cost as unknown, `cost.usd: null`, where it read a known $0. Each answer of such a model is written with no cost total, as a harness run's unpriced answer already was, so one such answer makes the run's cost unknown, a compaction's summary included. A model the catalog lists at $0, and a set price of `0,0`, stay a known $0. Priced models are unchanged.
- A run whose `file_ids` holds something that is not a file id is now told what it sent and where a file id comes from: `file_ids[0] is "", not a file id: use the file_id an upload answered (POST /v2/files answers it under file.file_id).`, where it said `file_ids[0] is a file id`. What was sent shows as its JSON, cut to 40 characters (never inside a surrogate pair). The README and llms.txt say the upload's answer holds the id under `file`.
- Uploads: a reservation that names no `content_type` takes its path's extension's type, as a run's files are served (`plan.md` is `text/markdown; charset=utf-8`; an unknown extension stays `application/octet-stream`; a named type still wins). `upload.expires_at`, which the contract requires, is documented as advisory and not enforced in this release: a later `PUT` is still taken. The refusals that point at an upload route name it in full: `POST /v2/files`, `PUT /v2/files/<id>`. The extension table resolves only its own extensions: a run's file named `x.constructor` or `x.__proto__` is listed with no type and served as `application/octet-stream`, where it was listed without a `content_type` and could not be read, and an upload so named takes its bytes.
- A start clears the temp files a crashed upload left: a file sent in one call leaves `.incoming.<pid>.<hex>.tmp` in `DIR/files/`, and a PUT leaves `bytes.<pid>.<hex>.tmp` in the file's folder. The sweep runs under the data directory's lock, before the server answers anything, so no upload of its own is in flight. It matches only those exact names and removes only regular files: a link named like one is not followed or removed, a `DIR/files/` that is itself a link is not swept at all, and a file's bytes and record stay. A start that removed any logs `event=uploads_swept removed=N`.
- The usage row (`Cost so far`) is always a run's last timeline row. It used to keep the place it first appeared, after the run's first answer, while its count and timestamp moved on with every answer after, so it sat above rows it postdated. When a row is served after it, it now moves to the end with a new `seq`, a change a cursor sees.
- The workspace walk no longer leaves out what it cannot look at. In a directory that lists but cannot be searched (mode 0444, say), each entry's own look failed and the walk skipped it without a word, so a caller read the directory as holding nothing and could infer its files were gone. Each such entry is now reported refused as `unreadable`, and the rest of the run's files are still walked. An entry removed between the listing and its look is still left out, as it is gone.
- A run's `run.ended` webhook is no longer lost, or sent twice under two ids, across a crash or a refused write. The server now chooses a run's end time once and gives it to the agent store, so the store's `ended_at` and the message's `webhook-id` name one time (`carryBack` takes it as `endedAt`, an ISO time in UTC; with none it stamps now, as before). `run.ended` is queued only once the agent store has the end, and the webhook outbox keeps which end of each run it queued in the same write as the message; a server that starts queues any end of the last day that the agent store holds and the outbox does not, under the same id, so neither a kill nor a crash of the machine loses it; the first start after this change records when it began (`DIR/end-notices-since`), and an end before it, or before the webhook was set, is never sent. Behaviour changes: while the agent store refuses a run's end, `run.ended` waits until the end is stored (a later try or the next server), where it used to go out at once, and a receiver that reads the run back on its message reads it ended; a run is continued only once its end's `run.ended` is in the outbox, `503` with a retry meanwhile, so the continued run's next end has its own id.
- A run that comes to need its person while no server is up now sends its `run.needs_input` once a server takes it over: the webhook outbox keeps which wait of each run it queued in the same write as the message, and a server that adopts a run queues a wait it stands at that the outbox does not name, under the wait's one `webhook-id` (its stop's, or its question's row's). A wait the previous server already queued is not sent again; one whose outbox write failed before a kill is queued again by the next server. A run waiting when a server is first upgraded to this has no record yet, so its `run.needs_input` is sent once more, under the id it had.
- What serve reads out of a run's workspace never goes through a link, and never reads a file hard-linked in. That covers the files endpoint (`GET /runs/:id/files/:path`), the file listing, and each skill's `SKILL.md` at a run's start.
  - Before, the file view checked each component with `lstat` and then opened the file by its path, so a name swapped for a link in between could hand it a file outside the workspace. The listing likewise `stat`ed by path.
  - Now every read goes through pi-durable-disk's confinement, which walks on held directory handles without following links. The path must be the workspace joined with its own names, so a link anywhere on the way is refused, even one pointing inside the workspace. Only regular files with a single link are read.
  - A file serve cannot read is listed with `unreadable: true` (a serve extension on `RunFile`), and reading it answers 403 `system_file_forbidden` with `details.reason` `unreadable`, never a 500. A tool in the run's box creates such a file when it makes it owner-only.
  - A directory the walk cannot read is reported as refused, never read as empty, and the workspace itself unread is an error (no working copy).
  - pi-durable-disk exports `Confinement`, its types (`Pin`, `PinSpec`, `RootName`), and its test seam as `confineTestHooks`, from `@parcha/pi-durable-disk/internals`.
- `GET /v2/setup/import` answers `200 { "offer": false }` where it answered `404`: to the signed-in owner when no import was asked for at setup, and to the setup page of a server that cannot import (a public address, or open setup off loopback). The console reads that answer as no offer, so its first page no longer logs a failed request in the browser; it still reads a `404` from an older server the same way. An offer, its count and an import's state answer as before, and a browser that is not signed in still gets `404` once the owner is set.
- A message sent after a stop reads as the person's next message in the timeline ("You: …"), never as a steer ("You (steer): …"). The timeline took any message right after a tool round as a steer into the running turn, but a stop or a cancel can cut a turn right there. The timeline now reads two marks a cut leaves:
  - an aborted call's result (pi-durable's `aborted` diagnostic);
  - the end mark of a model request that had left and was aborted (`agentrun.request_ended`, neither `unsent` nor a compaction's).

  After either of these, the next message starts a new turn, in the stored view and the live one alike. A message placed between a tool round and the next request is still a steer.

  agentrun-serve's README, `llms.txt`, the serve skill and the MCP tools `runs_respond` and `runs_cancel` now say what can be continued. A stopped run can: a follow-up starts a fresh turn on its history, and the cut turn is not replayed. A cancelled run cannot: a message to it is `409 not_continuable`, so start a new run.
- A Claude or Codex turn the person stopped stays stopped when the run's process is replaced. Before, the next process found the stopped turn still open in the session and resumed it: it ran its tools and paid for its model requests again, and its answer came before the person's follow-up. Now serve's open reads the run's stop record and settles that turn, ended `interrupted`, with nothing sent, and the follow-up is a turn of its own. A turn a crash or a drain cut is resumed as before.
  - A stop that lands before the harness took the person's message withdraws that message too, so it is never sent later: not with the follow-up in the same process, and not by a process that replaces it.
  - `AdapterSession` has `settleOpenTurn()`: it records the open turn as ended, interrupted, withdraws the input the turn never placed, and sends nothing. The Claude Agent, Codex and faux adapters implement it. A harness that runs as a child process does both with `settleTurn(drive)` from `@parcha/agentrun-kernel/process`. An adapter written outside this repository without it keeps the old behaviour: its stopped turn is resumed.
  - After `interrupt()`, the Claude Agent and Codex sessions read `open_turn` while the stopped turn is still open in their records. serve's stop settles it there; a runner's pause leaves it for the next process to resume.
  - When the earlier process died before it wrote the stopped turn's end, the next one writes it, `interrupted`, before the follow-up's turn starts.
- The person's answer to an agent's question is no longer shown as a steer. `ask_person` ends its turn, so the answer is the person's next message, but the timeline labeled every message that came straight after a tool result `You (steer): ...`. The timeline gains `registerTurnEnding(tool)`, for a host's tool that ends its turn, and serve registers `ask_person` with it. A message after a round whose tools all end the turn reads `You: ...`; a steer into a running turn reads as before. A call to such a tool that failed (an agent asking with no `ask_person` bound gets an error and goes on) ends nothing, so a message after it is still a steer.
- A run's timeline only grows: a row it has shown stays, in its place, across a woken run's processes. A woken run rebuilds its live rows from the conversation's current view, which after a compaction no longer holds the messages the summary replaced, so those messages and their answers used to disappear from `/v2/runs/{id}/timeline`. Only live rows (a stream, a wait, a run phase) are dropped when they end. `timelineReducer` gains `isLive(id)`, which tells such a row from one a committed entry made. The server remembers which rows were shown live over every connection to a run, so a wait that ended while the connection was down goes with the next snapshot, never staying beside newer work.
- A run's tool counts read a call's outcome as its timeline does, and analytics name each tool in plain words. `runMetricsOf` counted a tool error only for an error result, so a refused or failed fetch (a result with `ok: false`) counted as a success while its row read failed. One rule, `callFailed(tool, result)` in the timeline's labels, now decides both. The label registry also keeps a tool's display name (`registerLabel(tool, label, fold?, { name })`, read with `nameOf`), and serve's `GET /v2/stats` sends it as `label` beside each `tools` row's `tool` (`stats/3`). The console shows the label, with the name the tool was called by as its title, so `monid_discover`, the browser's `run`, `browser_release` and `submit` read "Search monid's catalog", "Open or act on a page in the browser", "Release the browser session" and "Submit the answer". monid's tools also get timeline labels of their own.
- A run's timeline names a file by its path in the run's workspace (`Wrote hello.md`, `Saved hello.md`), even when the call named it by an absolute path there, as the Claude CLI does. Before, a claude-agent run's write rows read `Wrote <data>/runs/<id>/work/hello.md`. A path outside the run's directory stays as the call gave it, and the call's arguments, its input preview and what the store keeps are unchanged.
  - `@parcha/pi-durable-agent`: `TimelineOptions.path`, how a row names a file path a call gave (`path`, `file_path`) or saved. Both the stored view (`timelineOf`) and the live one (`timelineReducer`) take it. Default: as given.
  - `renderText` takes the same `path` option for folded calls ("Read 2 files: a.md, b.md"), and a fold spec says which of its subjects are file paths (`FoldSpec.path`; read, write and edit set it). The input preview stays the call's own.
  - serve names a row's file as a whole path against the run's `work/`, by `path.relative`: relative to it inside the run's directory (`hello.md`, `../work 2024/a.md`), and exactly as given elsewhere.
- `GET /v2/settings/tool-keys` lists in each key's `unlocks` only what this server binds, as capabilities show it: the tools of `tools`, and a decision provider of `workflows.decisions.providers` as `decisions.<id>`; `harnesses` is the union of those tools' harnesses. The Parallel key no longer promises `web_search`; a name in the key table shows once the server binds it, as the monid tools do on pi.
- A run now ends on the record its turn writes as it ends, never on the order of its timeline rows or on when its door last read idle. On pi that record is the settlement of the person's message, which pi-durable writes in the commit of the turn's answer. On claude-agent and codex it is the bridge's turn end, now written in the commit of the turn's last entries, naming the message it took and the entry of its answer. A message the run took stays open until its turn's end closes it, so a follow-up queued behind a turn keeps the run open after a crash and a server restart too.

  What this changes:
  - A turn that ends showing no text completes the run with `result.final_answer` `""`, never an earlier turn's answer. That covers a pi stop with only a thinking block or no content, a Claude `/compact`, a cost-only request, and two parallel calls that fail before the turn stops. Before, some of these runs read `working` until they were cancelled.
  - A turn the harness ends interrupted on its own, with no stop or cancel asked for, leaves the run `input_required`: it waits for the next message instead of reading `working` for good.
  - A run's first terminal status is its last. An agentic run reads `cancelled` from the cancel's 202 on, and nothing its cut turn writes later, an answer or a cost over its cap, moves it to `completed` or `failed`. A server that took the cancel and went down before it carried the run's end back leaves the next server to end it `cancelled` too.
  - A claude-agent or codex turn whose process died before its end was written ends as the run's records show it on the next open: failed when its error is there, completed when its answer is, and otherwise interrupted, so the run waits for the person (`input_required`) instead of completing with `""`.
  - A run's status is its last turn's end: a turn that fails with a message queued behind it leaves the run open, and when the queued turn completes the run completes with its answer, the failed turn kept in its timeline.
  - A server restart that drains a claude-agent or codex turn mid-way no longer reads that turn as stopped: the next process resumes it, and the messages that were waiting are sent then. pi-durable-disk's door tells its hook why an abort comes (`reason`: `request` or `stop`).
  - `GET /runs/{id}` has `last_progress_at`: while a message is open, when its turn last wrote anything, so a run working through a long tool phase shows whether it is moving.
  - pi-durable-disk's door `GET /events?inputs=1` adds `inputs` to its snapshot: pi's input submissions from the newest settled one on, so a server adopting a live pi run knows how its newest turn ended. Without the parameter the stream is as before.

  No agentic run ends on its rows any more. A run whose records hold no turn end, an import or a workflow, still reads its answer from its rows as before. A claude-agent or codex message an older server sent and wrote no end for is closed by the run's next process, which writes that turn's end as it opens; until then a live process of an older server keeps its run open.
- `web_fetch` fetches a page once a run. The first call for a URL reads the page, through Parallel's extract or the direct fetch, and the run keeps what it read of its last 8 pages, by the URL asked for: every later part of that URL comes from it, with no request. Through Parallel, each part used to be one more billed extract, asked for the page's text up to that part's end with no ceiling. Now a page costs one extract a run, asked for its first 1,000,001 characters, and its parts never mix the two providers' text. A page is read to its first 1,000,000 characters from either provider. An `offset` at or past them is refused before anything is fetched, and the last part a read reaches of a longer page has `beyond_read`. A page's links left out past their caps are counted, `links_omitted`.
- On OpenRouter (OPENROUTER_API_KEY alone, or a model named `openrouter/<vendor>/...`), `web_search` on `auto` no longer picks Codex's own search, which OpenRouter does not run: a codex agent searches through Parallel on the server's key, else keyless Exa, and every model request is priced. A pick of `native` there resolves `unavailable: no native search for codex on OpenRouter` (likewise for claude-agent), saying why.
- **serve's box archive test writes a later box's record only once the start sweep has marked the old one**
  - The test wrote the later record right after a `GET /runs`, which is answered while the start sweep still settles. A sweep still inside its reap of the old record then wrote that record's reaped mark over the new one, so the archive found a record already let go and reaped nothing.
  - It now waits for the sweep's mark on disk before writing the later record, as a run process would (no run starts before the sweep is done), and checks that the record the archive marks is the later box's own.
- Two launcher tests in `secret-custody.test.mjs` read what the launcher said once its process ends on `close`, not `exit`. `exit` can come before the child's stderr pipe is read to its end, so the refusal of secrets past a pipe's 4096 bytes could read as an empty string and fail the test intermittently.
- serve's recovery tests wait on what they wait for, not on a count of polls. A server started in a process of its own is ready when it prints its banner (its address and key lines), and one that exits before that fails the test at once with what it printed; a run is working, adopted or stopped when its timeline, long-polled, says so, and one that ends in another status fails the test at once. Before, both waits gave up after a fixed number of 50 ms polls (about 10 s for a server, about 15 s for a run), which a slow start on a loaded machine could pass, failing a test whose behaviour was right.
- Code that was written more than once in serve and the timeline now has one copy, with no change to any request, answer or message:
  - serve builds the test switches a run's process gets once, at start, instead of passing each one through the run manager as a separate option. `startServe`'s options are unchanged.
  - One helper builds the "The body keeps no field" refusal that 8 routes wrote by hand; one per-key queue serves both uploads' commits and the learning folder; one wait-until-deadline serves both waits in a run's start; the inline file types come from the same table as the file types; the install hint for a harness that cannot load is built in one place for claude-agent and codex; the count of a workflow run's answered llm calls is one function for its record and its cost.
  - The timeline reads a tool result's text only for a tool that reads its outcome from that text.
  - A recovered run with nothing learned no longer removes two paths that are not there.

  One wait ends sooner, and nothing else about it changes: a stop that waits for a run's rows to catch up wakes when the rows change, not on the next 100 ms tick. It still ends at the same deadline.
- A test pins a stop across a restart of a compacted run, end to end. The run compacts and its turn is stopped, then the server closes and the next server reads the run from the compacted snapshot.
